#5232. Ether ETFs Shed $139.7M on November 2, Extending a Difficult Run for Fund Flows

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: US spot Ether ETFs saw approximately $139.7 million in net outflows on November 2, Farside Investors’ daily data show. The amounts are published for specific trading dates and can be adjusted by data providers.

On November 2, BlackRock’s ETHA was responsible for roughly $138.3 million of the reported net withdrawal. That clustering suggests the fund-level series deserves careful reading. Investors can track one issuer and the whole category without treating the two as interchangeable, particularly when other funds show smaller and sometimes opposite daily movements.

US spot Ether ETFs saw approximately $139.7 million in net outflows on November 2, Farside Investors’ daily data show. The previous listed sessions were also negative, with approximately $155.7 million leaving on October 30 and $105.5 million on October 29. The sequence offers a more informative starting point than any single day, although the scale of redemptions varies from session to session. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

ETH ETF flows are subscriptions and withdrawals through an investment wrapper, not a direct count of ETH sold by everyone in the ecosystem. Exchange traders, staking participants, derivatives desks and users of Ethereum applications have different motives and do not all appear in the same table. ETF holdings reflect a familiar securities-market channel that may lag or diverge from blockchain activity.

A series of negative sessions can change how investors talk about Ethereum exposure, especially when a large issuer accounts for most of a particular day’s redemptions. But a falling ETF share count and weaker network fundamentals are not synonymous. Analysts should compare flows with changes in broader risk appetite, fund allocations and the health of Ethereum-based services rather than assume one metric explains the others.

Ethereum has multiple sources of demand and risk: trading, staking, stablecoin settlement, application fees and long-term treasury allocation. A change in one part of that system should be tested against the others instead of promoted into a universal explanation. The market often overreacts when one publicly available metric is mistaken for the whole network economy.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on an earlier Ethereum ETF outflow comparison and the October Bitcoin and Ether ETF weekly review. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

The amounts are published for specific trading dates and can be adjusted by data providers. They do not tell us who redeemed, whether positions were hedged or whether the cash moved to Bitcoin, other funds or unrelated assets. Nor does this table establish that protocol staking withdrawals caused the ETF activity; those are different processes with distinct timing and constraints.

For investors, the right comparison is between liquidity, expected holding period and obligations. Staked ETH, ETF shares and treasury holdings may all track the same underlying asset, but their exit routes and operating constraints differ. A useful report therefore asks not simply how much ETH is involved, but how readily the exposure can be changed when conditions deteriorate.

The next useful evidence is the issuer breakdown over several additional sessions, alongside spot-market depth and whether redemption pressure broadens. A return to sustained net creations would change the recent trend, but an isolated inflow would not erase the larger pattern. Investors should keep ETF demand separate from claims about Ethereum’s throughput, staking or rollup adoption.

#5231. Hyperliquid Whale Refuses to Sell 35 Million XRP as Market Slumps: Main Crypto News This Morning

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A Hyperliquid whale holds a $44 million long on 35 million XRP, nearly 80% of large-wallet XRP longs, with liquidation at $0.9626, about 23% below the current price.

Crypto liquidations reached $386.42 million in 24 hours, 88% of them longs, led by Bitcoin ($100.74 million) and Ethereum ($49.31 million).

US spot Bitcoin ETFs saw $398.62 million in net daily outflows, led by Fidelity’s FBTC ($164 million), while 44,857 ETH left spot Ethereum ETFs.

Binance posted a record $15.4 billion weekly net outflow following a $6.3 billion hack the FBI attributed to North Korea.

This week’s key US triggers are the midterm elections and JOLTS (Tuesday), ISM services (Wednesday), the Fed balance sheet (Thursday) and Nonfarm Payrolls (Friday).

The crypto market greeted Tuesday morning, November 3, with a massive wave of margin position closures. Over the past 24 hours, the positions of 94,997 traders were forcibly liquidated for a total of $386.42 million, with 88% of all losses ($339.92 million) falling on long contracts, while Bitcoin ($100.74 million) and Ethereum ($49.31 million) led in liquidation volume as per CoinGlass.

A strong bearish skew persists in the US spot ETF sector: the net daily outflow from BTC funds amounted to $398.62 million, with the main blow falling on the Fidelity fund (FBTC), which saw $164 million leave. Over the day, 44,857 ETH were withdrawn from spot Ethereum ETFs. The combined assets under management (AUM) of all crypto ETFs stand at $95.83 billion as per SoSoValue.

The current daily outflow has spread across the entire line of exchange-traded products: SOL funds lost 129.19 thousand coins, outflows from XRP products amounted to 128.54 thousand tokens, and the specialized HYPE and ZEC funds shed 85.07 thousand and 13.53 thousand coins, respectively. Major operators are preemptively pulling liquidity out of volatile instruments ahead of the US midterm elections.

Amid the decline in market capitalization, a sharp split among participants has emerged: while retail investors are locking in losses en masse and most whales are betting on a decline, some large on-chain operators are opening aggressive counter-trend positions on decentralized exchanges.

The main event of the morning session was the actions of an unknown major player on the Hyperliquid platform, who, despite the overall market decline (NEAR dropped to $3.79, BNB to $697.67), stubbornly holds an XRP long worth tens of millions of dollars, turning the contract into the main barometer of local altcoin resilience.

The decentralized futures platform Hyperliquid is currently accumulating peak institutional volumes. At the same time, on-chain analytics of top wallets with balances of $1 million or more shows a strong bearish skew in open interest.

Of these wallets' total open positions of $4.5 billion, short contracts (shorts) account for $3.08 billion. Their long volume is limited to a modest $1.46 billion.

Leviathan addresses with positions of $50 million or more are almost unanimously betting on a further market decline. Against this grim backdrop, the long position of wallet '0x31b1ecf848443472e34d204a134812d86ab01434' remains the largest and riskiest counter-trend position in the system.

The whale holds 35.03 million XRP tokens. The notional value of the open position is $44.05 million at 10x Cross leverage.

The average entry price is $1.1298. At the peak of the morning momentum, the whale's unrealized profit exceeded $4.47 million, which amounts to more than 101% net return on the margin capital invested.

At the same time, the account's overall result is far from ideal: due to overall asset volatility, this wallet's net PnL over the past 30 days has fallen by $8.45 million.

The critical threshold in this whole story remains the forced liquidation price. The automatic position closure threshold sits at $0.9626.

The current XRP market price of around $1.2558 leaves the trader a buffer of about 23% to the liquidation point.

Large wallets' XRP positions on Hyperliquid are also skewed toward the bears. Against long positions of $55.70 million stands a massive wall of shorts worth $80.93 million. Nearly 80% of these longs belong to a single whale.

This morning's news backdrop points to a massive and painful restructuring of the entire market infrastructure. The central theme remains a severe liquidity crisis on centralized exchanges.

$6.3 billion Binance hack and the FBI's position: The agency officially attributed the attack to North Korean state cyber units. An analysis by lawyers at Reed Smith and Lathrop GPM showed that the exchange's insurance policies will not cover the damage. Standard D&O insurance protects only top management, while war exclusion clauses fully rule out payouts in the event of cyberwarfare waged by foreign states.

Record outflow of $15.4 billion: The weekly net capital outflow from Binance hit an all-time high. This volume exceeds the individual reserves of Bybit or Bitfinex and surpasses the combined balance of Gate, Bitget and KuCoin, which keeps the price of BNB at local lows.

Binance founder Changpeng Zhao's loud statements about rising risks to the financial system from automated AI-powered cyberattacks came amid the incident at the exchange he created, which showed the ineffectiveness of classic defense mechanisms against coordinated cyber operations. Zhao stressed that commercial banks, due to outdated IT architecture, are more vulnerable than crypto platforms.

Against this backdrop, decentralized protocols are confidently expanding their institutional presence. Hyperliquid's annual revenue has reached $713 million, and trading volume in perpetuals on stocks and commodities via HIP-3 has exceeded $500 billion. Building on these technologies, the operator of the New York Stock Exchange (ICE) and Kraken are already preparing to launch regulated futures in the US with White House support.

Meanwhile, serious changes are also taking place at the level of the largest L1 ecosystems. House of Stake (NEAR Protocol) participants approved the deflationary initiative HSP-029 with 99.96% of the vote, reducing annual emissions from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months. Daily issuance of new tokens is being cut from 89,500 to 57,300, while amid the general market decline the asset briefly dropped to $3.79.

Skepticism is meanwhile growing in the Ripple camp: the company reports that issuance of its dollar token RLUSD has surpassed $1 billion through partnerships with trust banks, but XRP holders are voicing open dissatisfaction. RLUSD is a centralized asset subject to freezing under US law, while XRP's decentralized status is not yet reflected in its market valuation.

At the same time, the Ethereum Foundation launched a platform for using the network as digital public infrastructure (DPI) for governments. Case studies on digital identity in Bhutan and confidential humanitarian aid systems have been published. At Devcon 8 in Mumbai, Vitalik Buterin called for developing verifiable code to minimize dependence on closed software and AI systems.

In the coming sessions, capital flows in the crypto market will be largely determined by external macroeconomic and political triggers. Investors and market makers need to keep the following critical factors in focus:

US Congressional midterm elections: The main political factor of this Tuesday, forcing large funds to preemptively close margin positions, cut leverage and hedge risks ahead of a change in the regulatory landscape.

Official JOLTS labor market report: Tuesday's release of job openings data will determine the short-term dynamics of the US dollar index and directly affect liquidity in high-risk assets.

ISM non-manufacturing business activity indices: Wednesday's statistics will highlight the real state of the US economy and shape the medium-term sentiment of major futures market operators.

Fed balance sheet on Thursday and Friday's Nonfarm Payrolls report: The key macroeconomic triggers of the week, which will lock in the final direction of capital flows through the end of the month.

#5230. Binance Draws $2.3 Billion Inflows After Record $6.3 Billion Hack

Home » Exchanges » Binance News » Binance Draws $2.3 Billion Inflows After Record $6.3 Billion Hack

Binance’s trading volume orchestrates a rapid comeback as $2.3 billion inflows hit the recovering platform.

Binance has swung back to net inflows of roughly $2.3 billion over 24 hours after beginning to restore customer access following a security breach estimated at roughly $6.3 billion, according to market-tracking data. The reversal came after a period of heavy withdrawals and reflects an early, if incomplete, return of confidence in the exchange.

The incident is being described as the largest theft in crypto’s history. Binance said its initial investigation found that a compromised laptop used by a DevOps engineer enabled unauthorized withdrawals of Bitcoin, Ether and other assets across multiple networks.

Efforts to contain the stolen assets have focused on tracing wallets and blocking transfers across the industry. Estimates of the amount frozen vary, but reports indicate that between roughly $2.7 billion and $3 billion of the funds were stopped or restricted shortly after the hacking incident.

JUST IN: 🚨 Binance net inflows flip positive as market activity surges.The exchange records a massive $2.3 billion in net inflows amid expanding user withdrawals.$BNB #Binance #Crypto

Ripple also said it froze $200 million in RLUSD connected to wallets linked to the suspected exploit. The intervention highlighted the role that issuers of centrally administered stablecoins can play in limiting the movement of funds after a major breach.

Binance has said it will cover customer losses. The exchange’s reopening of withdrawals appears to have been the critical test: hourly deposits began exceeding withdrawals as access expanded, and Binance led centralized exchanges in net inflows during the latest 24-hour period.

BNB sold off sharply after the hack, at one point falling about 8.7% within an hour. The token was hit harder than several other large cryptocurrencies because of its direct connection to the Binance ecosystem, even though the reported exploit did not target BNB Chain itself.

Futures positioning also turned more defensive, with funding rates slipping negative as traders increased short exposure. BNB later found support near its 200-day moving average, but it remains below pre-hack levels and faces resistance around the $733 area cited by technical analysts.

The broader crypto market has stayed under pressure, with Bitcoin near $77,000 and several major altcoins recording steeper monthly declines. Risk appetite has been strained by exchange-traded fund outflows, geopolitical concerns and uncertainty around monetary policy, magnifying the shock from the Binance breach.

A quick teaser: the number at the bottom of this page has a “$” in front of it, and it is not a typo. Google Gemini AI predicts a move for Cardano (ADA) that would see it trade at nearly 40 times its current price. Read on to see how Charles Hoskinson’s ADA crypto token could reach such an insane price.

For this outlandish prediction to hit, Gemini AI believes a hypothetical landmark catalyst would need to land for Cardano, acknowledging just how unlikely the event is.

For context, ADA is trading below $0.189 on Tuesday (November 3), after losing a key support zone the previous day, and it has had a rough October, down -22.2%. This is a “what if” scenario, and it assumes the following.

(SOURCE: Google Gemini AI Predicts a Wild Cardano Rally)

Late in Q4, a consortium of major central banks and Tier-1 financial institutions announces a pilot for wholesale tokenized bond settlement.

After a bake-off, they pick Cardano as the settlement layer, citing built-in compliance that doesn’t depend on bolt-on smart contract logic.

Within days, a US government digital asset reserve discloses that it has quietly accumulated ADA as a settlement-layer strategic asset. Spot ADA ETF inflows go parabolic. Short sellers learn what the word “squeeze” means.

On the weekly chart, ADA has spent a long stretch trapped in a descending channel, with levels at $0.41, $0.78, $1.18, and eventually $3 in play if it breaks out.

In this scenario, the Zurich Accord headline blasts ADA through the $0.25 breakout zone on enormous volume, which flips the channel’s upper boundary from resistance to support.

Each of those historical supply zones then becomes a stepping stone instead of a ceiling: $0.41, then $0.78, then $1.18. Once price clears the $3.09–$3.10 all-time high, there’s no overhead resistance at all, which is the textbook setup for price discovery.

Momentum indicators go deeply overbought and stay overbought, as they famously do in parabolic phases. Fair warning: today’s actual chart is bearish, with ADA slipping out of the ascending channel that carried it for months. Bulls need to reclaim $0.23 before this fantasy even gets off the ground.

Drumroll, please. Take today’s ~$0.19, add a central-bank-grade settlement win, a government reserve buyer, ETF mania, and a short squeeze for the ages, and here is the one-in-a-million, deeply fictional price target for January 1, 2027: ADA: $6.90

That would put ADA more than double its previous all-time high and imply a market cap north of $250Bn. It’s pure entertainment, not financial advice. The probability is about the same as finding a winning lottery ticket in your couch, but stranger things have happened.

#5228. Onlend Unveils No-Code Infrastructure to Launch Custom Onchain Lending Apps

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

Built by a team with experience scaling a leading DeFi lending protocol, Onlend aims to make it easier for fintechs, asset managers, curators, and asset issuers to build and operate their own lending products.

Onlend has publicly unveiled its decentralised infrastructure platform designed to let anyone create, launch, and operate their own onchain lending applications without building the underlying technology from scratch.

The project is entering a market with considerable room for expansion. Global debt surpassed $350 trillion in early 2026, according to the Institute of International Finance, while active loans across DeFi protocols currently stand at approximately $33 billion, according to DefiLlama. Although the figures measure different markets, they illustrate the relatively small scale of onchain lending today.

At the same time, the infrastructure supporting onchain financial activity continues to expand. The stablecoin market has grown beyond $300 billion, while the value of tokenized real-world assets has reached approximately $39 billion, creating new opportunities for lending products built around these assets.

However, launching such products remains challenging. Existing lending protocols offer established markets but can limit how teams configure and operate their lending use cases. Building custom infrastructure, meanwhile, requires significant engineering resources and development time.

Onlend addresses this gap with a permissionless, no-code platform that allows anyone to launch lending applications tailored to their own use-cases, users, and operational requirements.

Through Onlend, builders can configure supported assets (with multi-collateral and multi-borrow optionality), collateral parameters, interest-rate models, price oracles, liquidation mechanisms, fees, and administrative permissions. Applications can operate with open or restricted access, use a customizable white-label interface, and remain actively managed or operate under immutable rules.

One of its main technical differentiators is support for advanced interest-rate models beyond traditional utilization-based curves. These include fixed rates, rates linked to external benchmarks such as staking yields, and targeted rates for specific borrowers.

The flexibility opens several potential applications across financial services.

For fintechs and neobanks, Onlend provides a way to introduce collateral-backed borrowing directly into their existing consumer applications, adding lending functionality without developing the feature from the ground up, nor relying on rigid third-party protocols.

Asset managers and curators can create and manage lending markets aligned with their strategies, retain control over market configurations, and capture revenue through the markets they operate.

Tokenized real-world asset issuers can establish dedicated lending markets using their assets as collateral, while stablecoin issuers, liquid staking protocols, and other DeFi projects can develop lending products tailored to their ecosystems.

Designed for the emerging agentic economy, where AI agents can autonomously lend and borrow capital to finance their operations.

The approach differs from established lending protocols such as Aave, where introducing new assets into governed markets involves an approval process, and Morpho, which enables permissionless market creation but uses immutable market parameters. Onlend combines permissionless application creation with broader customization, ongoing management options, and branded interfaces.

Behind the project is a team with experience at Flowdesk, MEV Capital, and Folks Finance. Its members have previously helped scale the lending protocol to more than $400 million in peak total value locked and $13 billion in cumulative deposits volume.

With initial testnet deployments targeted for Q4 2026, Onlend is positioning itself as infrastructure for a new generation of lending applications, where teams can control their financial products rather than depend entirely on existing lending markets.

More information is available at onlend.io, and official X and Linkedin profiles.

#5227. Ethereum exchange reserves hit 2016 low – As Bitmine adds another $11.22M ETH

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Ethereum [ETH] exchange reserves dropped to their lowest levels since 2016, raising hopes of a supply-driven recovery despite the prevailing market weakness.

According to CryptoQuant Community insights, the total ETH exchange reserves declined to roughly 14.5 million ETH on November 2. This decline reportedly marked a 58.6% reduction from the 2020 peak of 35 million ETH.

Over that period, the exchanges have in fact lost around 20.5 million ETH from their cumulative balances.

Notably, Binance registered a similar drop, with reserves falling 37.1% from their 2025 peak of 4.9 million ETH, with its holdings reaching 3.08 million ETH, revisiting levels last seen in late 2020.

It is also worth noting that the reserve drop also coincided with reports of Binance’s October 29 hack, which allegedly caused significant losses.

For context, these security concerns could have encouraged investors to transfer their holdings into self-custody wallets, rather than necessarily indicating stronger accumulation or renewed buying demand.

Institutional accumulation has also strengthened despite the token’s recent struggles, with Tom Lee’s Bitmine expanding its ETH holdings.

Lookonchain recently reported on X that Bitmine purchased another 5,000 ETH valued roughly $11.22 million, bringing the total acquisition to 6.065 million ETH, worth nearly $13.7 billion.

The institution now controls around 4.96% of Ethereum’s total supply, strengthening its significant exposure to the asset.

Meanwhile, CoinGlass analytics highlighted that Ethereum’s Spot netflows remained negative, registering approximately $7.91 million in net outflows at press time.

Bitmine’s continued purchases, together with the negative Spot netflows, reinforced the declining immediate selling pressure picture.

Traders on the derivatives market also showed renewed optimism despite Ethereum’s recent market weakness. In particular, ETH’s OI-Weighted Funding Rate had recovered to 0.0101% at press time, returning to positive territory after an earlier brief negative reading.

This recovery implied that long traders were paying shorts, which reflected fresh demand for bullish leveraged positions.

Besides, Binance’s top trader Long/Short Ratio revealed a considerable preference for long positions, with the long accounts representing 70.2% of top traders, compared to 29.8% holding short positions.

These readings together suggested that market participants were increasingly optimistic for price recovery despite the prevailing downward trend.

Ethereum’s defense of the $2,243 support level validated the hopes of a price rebound despite the prevailing bearish market outlook.

Following the defense, Ethereum’s market value sat at $2,254 as of writing time, positioning itself around the middle of its descending channel.

More importantly, the RSI indicator stood at 33.23, approaching oversold conditions and raising the possibility of selling exhaustion.

The Money Flow Index (MFI) meanwhile reached 37.18, highlighting relatively weak capital inflows despite the successful defense of the support level.

Overall, Ethereum’s declining exchange reserves, Bitmine accumulation, and bullish derivatives positioning favored the possibility of a rebound.

A continued buying above $2,243 could strengthen that outlook, while reclaiming the $2,385 resistance would provide stronger confirmation of a sustained recovery.

#5226. 131 Million ADA Delegated to Dead Cardano Pools, Not Earning

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

According to popular Cardano explorer Cexplorer there are over 131 million ADA currently delegated to retired stake pools, leaving thousands of delegators without staking rewards.

Cardano staking pools offer ADA holders a way to passively earn rewards for securing the blockchain by delegating their coins to network validators, all without locking up or losing control of their funds.

Cexplorer recently posted on X that 59,025 delegators still have their ADA delegated to pools that have retired. Therefore, they are not earning staking rewards on their current delegations.

⚠️ Over 131M $ADA is currently delegated to retired stake pools. 59,025 delegators staking with those pools are not earning any staking rewards. If you're one of them, consider choosing a new stake pool to keep earning rewards and supporting Cardano's decentralization. https://t.co/L9mitriW2P

According to Cexplorer data, 131.63 million ADA is still delegated to retired pools and the number of retired pools is given as 2,720. Holders can delegate ADA to a stake pool and earn rewards as long as the pool they choose is active, which contributes to network security and decentralization.

However, when a stake pool retires, delegators must select another active pool if they want to continue earning rewards. Their ADA does not disappear simply because the pool has retired, but the delegation no longer provides the expected staking benefits.

Users who are delegating their ADA to such retired pools are urged to review their staking arrangements and consider moving their ADA to active stake pools.

Major crypto exchange Binance has commenced phased withdrawals for crypto networks days after it suffered a hack that impacted nearly $6.3 billion in client assets. In line with this, Binance will reopen withdrawals for Cardano (ADA) on the BSC network beginning at 04:00 (UTC) on November 3, 2026.

In Dijkstra upgrade news, Node 11.2 is out as a pre-release. It carries the full Dijkstra hard fork feature set apart from Leios and will move DijkstraNet into the Dijkstra era so downstream teams can start integration testing.

Node 11.3 includes Leios and is set to deliver the complete Dijkstra feature set. A tagged pre-release is realistically three to four weeks away, two at the earliest.

#5225. Binance Adds More Withdrawal Routes on November 3 After Security Checks

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Binance’s November 3 security-recovery notice set out a wider withdrawal restart for additional tokens and networks beginning around 04:00 UTC. Binance’s announcement is a company statement, not an independent certification that all systems are secure.

The subtle point is that a route reopening is a narrow, testable milestone. It is stronger than a vague promise of normal service, yet smaller than a comprehensive incident resolution. Customers trying to move funds should confirm the correct network at the moment they initiate a transaction, because an asset ticker alone is an incomplete withdrawal instruction.

Binance’s November 3 security-recovery notice set out a wider withdrawal restart for additional tokens and networks beginning around 04:00 UTC. The company is reopening routes progressively after its late-October hot-wallet incident rather than restoring every asset in one operation. Some supported routes run over Ethereum, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP Ledger and other networks, with availability determined by the exact token-and-chain combination. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

A customer withdrawal has several moving parts: the platform must hold the asset, approve the destination route, sign the transaction safely and be able to reconcile the result. A token can exist on multiple chains, so announcing a network reopening does not necessarily make every representation of that asset withdrawable. Binance’s route lists and live withdrawal interface, rather than a broad press headline, are the practical source for supported combinations.

The update matters because access to customer assets is the operational test after a security event. Replenishing balances is one question; returning funds through secure, reliable systems is another. A phased process may be frustrating to users, but it allows the operator to validate infrastructure and monitor transactions before widening access. It is also why reported recovery progress should not be confused with universal service restoration.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

This development also connects to two subjects our newsroom has been following: Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. They explain related market structure and should not be mistaken for evidence that every new proposal is already live.

Binance’s announcement is a company statement, not an independent certification that all systems are secure. It does not mean every customer, jurisdiction or network has identical withdrawal access. Users should ignore unsolicited messages claiming to fast-track withdrawals and use only the official app or website. Transaction fees, identity checks and asset-specific restrictions may still apply.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Look for updated token-and-network lists, completed withdrawal confirmations and explanations of any routes that remain restricted. The quality of incident follow-up will also depend on technical disclosures and the effectiveness of the company’s controls after reopening. For now, the support notice is best read as a dated expansion of eligible routes, not an all-clear for the entire platform.

#5224. After 24 Million Launches, Just 312 Memecoins Are Above $100,000

Home » Altcoins » After 24 Million Launches, Just 312 Memecoins Are Above $100,000

The rarest breed of meme currencies launched on Pump.Fun stay above a $100,000 market capitalization.

Pump.fun’s explosive memecoin-creation activity is colliding with a stark outcome: of roughly 24 million launches on the platform, only 312 tokens have climbed above a $100,000 valuation threshold. The gap has sharpened scrutiny of how much lasting value the memecoin launchpad is producing.

The numbers underscore the extreme attrition built into rapid-fire token launches. Vast volumes of new memecoins can create attention and trading bursts, but only a tiny fraction appear to develop enough liquidity, demand or staying power to clear even relatively modest valuation levels.

Pump.fun has become closely associated with the low-cost, high-speed creation of memecoins. That model can lower barriers for creators and traders, while also producing an overwhelming supply of projects competing for the same pool of capital.

JUST IN: THERE HAVE BEEN 24.4M COINS LAUNCHED ON PUMPFUN312 ARE CURRENTLY ABOVE $100K MARKET CAP https://t.co/t0Qvv6Oqj3

With only 312 tokens reportedly surpassing $100,000 out of 24 million launches, the implied success rate is exceptionally small. The figure does not by itself establish whether individual launches were designed for long-term development, short-term trading or experimentation, but it highlights how quickly most tokens fade from view.

The DeFi platform is now facing broader questions about whether transaction activity and launch counts translate into a durable business model. High turnover may generate engagement, yet it can also leave traders exposed to thin liquidity and sharp price reversals once early interest disappears.

Concerns around memecoins have been amplified by the weak performance of PUMP amid softer market sentiment. When a platform-linked token struggles during a risk-off period, investors tend to look more closely at the underlying economics: user retention, trading demand and the quality of projects emerging from the ecosystem.

Pump.fun’s figures offer a blunt reminder for the wider crypto market: token creation is easy, but building liquidity and relevance is not. Investors weighing exposure to launchpad ecosystems will likely focus less on raw volume and more on whether those platforms can consistently produce assets that survive beyond the first burst of speculation.

Strategy is continuing to increase the size of its Bitcoin [BTC] treasury. This comes with the latest addition of 811 BTC during the last week of October and the first week of November.

This purchase took its total holdings to 849,327 Bitcoins. Strategy also purchased $208 million of STRC. This demonstrated additional utilization of the company’s capital structure to fund both shareholder and treasury-related activities.

It’s worth noting that Strategy still holds $4.9 billion in USD, providing liquidity for future purchases and financial obligations.

By controlling over 4% of the total supply of Bitcoin in circulation today, Strategy is limiting the number of coins available for purchase by all market participants.

While Strategy may continue to add to its existing inventory of BTC and create greater institutional demand for them. Moreover, it will also have an increasing degree of sensitivity to changes in their value due to its increased exposure.

Strategy’s declining leverage demonstrates the changing financial condition of Strategy as its Bitcoin treasury has grown. At the beginning of January 2023, the strategy’s overall leverage was approximately 108%.

As such, Strategy was then more sensitive to its obligation to repay debt compared to its current reserves.

However, by November, Strategy’s overall leverage had declined to just 3%. Even though Strategy held significantly more Bitcoin at that time, the decline in leverage is a reflection of a much healthier balance sheet on the part of Strategy.

These resources give it more room to manage debt and fund operations without immediately selling Bitcoin.

Thus, Strategy will be able to accumulate Bitcoin through the entire range of prices. This includes down markets and maintaining the demand for Bitcoin among institutions while minimizing its own exposure to price fluctuations.

Strategy has continued to develop its treasury strategy. However, safety of custody for institutional investors is a major component of increasing investor demand.

Institutional investors that have large holdings of Bitcoins need to be able to protect their investments by taking measures to avoid loss through theft or unauthorized transactions.

Many institutional investors now use cold storage systems and multi-signature wallet structures. The multi-signature system requires consent of more than two parties before any transaction can occur.

Safety and transparency of custody and reporting will be key in maintaining the confidence of institutional investors in Bitcoin. Those elements could also be used to help sustain the interest of institutional investors regardless of the current market conditions.

Strategy currently holds 849,327 BTC in its treasury, representing more than 4% of all the Bitcoins that exist.

Strategy cut leverage to 3%, but custody risks could threaten institutional demand.

#5220. Binance Publishes New Proof-of-Reserves Snapshot Following October Attack

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

TL;DR: Binance said on November 2 that it had completed a zero-knowledge proof-of-reserves procedure for a snapshot taken October 31, following the exchange’s late-October security incident. Proof of reserves should not be described as a full financial audit, a guarantee against losses or evidence that all stolen assets have been returned.

In the aftermath of a major exploit, balance verification and service recovery are parallel tasks. Reserve attestations may restore visibility, while withdrawal readbacks show that the platform can operate. Neither should substitute for a full account of the breach. Reading the three separately makes it harder for one reassuring number to obscure unresolved questions.

Binance said on November 2 that it had completed a zero-knowledge proof-of-reserves procedure for a snapshot taken October 31, following the exchange’s late-October security incident. Its announcement listed ten major cryptoassets, including Bitcoin, Ether, USDT, BNB, Solana, USDC, XRP and several dollar tokens. The company said the affected asset replenishment programme had reached approximately 95% at the time of the update. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

A proof-of-reserves calculation tests whether a set of exchange-controlled assets can support the included customer balances under the method and date specified. Zero-knowledge techniques can help demonstrate a relationship without exposing every account balance. That is useful, but it does not automatically assess unrelated corporate liabilities, future cash flows, operational security or the completeness of every entity in a corporate group.

Publishing a new snapshot gives customers a concrete checkpoint rather than asking them to accept an undated reassurance. The advantage is comparability over time if disclosures remain consistent and complete. Yet a positive proof on October 31 cannot certify a November 3 withdrawal or prevent another attack. The most meaningful assessment combines reserve information with functioning services, incident disclosure and independent scrutiny.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. A traced wallet is not automatically frozen; a frozen balance is not automatically returned; and a reopened withdrawal route is not a completed security audit. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

For additional perspective, it is worth reading our existing reporting on Binance’s earlier staged withdrawal restart and Bitcoin payment infrastructure and node software context. Those background reports help separate established facts from a new, still-developing announcement.

Proof of reserves should not be described as a full financial audit, a guarantee against losses or evidence that all stolen assets have been returned. The company’s 95% replenishment claim refers to affected asset coverage, not an identical ratio for every token. Liquidity and availability can differ by asset even when an aggregate recovery figure appears strong.

Operational controls matter most when a business is under stress. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Binance said another snapshot would follow on November 4 and that the related proof process could take roughly 40 to 50 hours. The important next step is to inspect the published scope, proof methodology and any changes in customer liabilities. Separate withdrawal updates will determine whether the reserves shown on paper translate into reliable customer access.

#5219. Binance hack: Fake job assignment helped attackers reach wallet systems, exchange says

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Read the best crypto stories of the day in less than 5 minutes. Join to get it daily in your inbox.

Binance says a fake recruitment assignment helped attackers gain access to employee computers before the October 29 hack. Its initial findings describe a campaign that eventually reached an internal service used to authorize wallet transactions, affecting approximately $6.34 billion in assets.

The November 3 update expands Binance’s earlier account of a compromised engineer’s laptop. It also explains why suspending withdrawals failed to stop every transfer: attackers had established permissions on Ethereum that required separate action to revoke.

According to Binance, a software engineer was approached through a legitimate job-search platform in September. Someone posing as a project director arranged interviews with a supposed HR representative and the CEO of a fictitious company.

Near the end of the second interview, the engineer was asked to download a coding assignment onto a work computer.

Binance says the assignment contained malicious software that ran during setup. It replaced part of a legitimate development tool and connected to an attacker-controlled server, escaping detection by the device’s security tools at the time.

The exchange also disclosed an earlier June incident involving a tampered software installation downloaded by another engineer. Binance says it replaced that device and changed related credentials, although the incident exposed information about its wallet infrastructure team.

On October 20, the attackers used the compromised computer and an internal AI tool to gain control of two colleagues’ computers, Binance said.

They discovered a credential allowing software deployment within the wallet infrastructure. On October 29, they used the stolen access to deploy software and launch the theft.

The attackers reached Binance’s wallet-signing service, enabling transfers outside the normal withdrawal process.

On Ethereum, they also used EIP-7702, a feature allowing a wallet to delegate certain functions to software. That permission let attacker-controlled code continue moving assets after Binance suspended withdrawals.

Binance says its wallet’s security design complicated emergency revocation. The team developed a specialized procedure and cleared the delegation at 00:10 UTC on October 30.

Binance says it has rebuilt affected wallet systems and its internal AI tool, replaced compromised devices, and disabled related credentials.

It reports approximately $2.85 billion frozen or intercepted, with the amount subject to reconciliation and changing prices.

The company says it has reserved more than enough funds to cover the loss and is purchasing outstanding affected assets. Independent security firms will review its initial findings as the investigation continues.

This is a reminder that incident response extends well beyond technical containment. Security teams investigate access paths, compliance teams track the assets, and customer-support teams must resolve the consequences for legitimate users. Each strand has a different evidence standard and timeline; treating them as a single success-or-failure metric misses much of the work.

The FBI said on October 31 that North Korean actors associated with the TraderTraitor operation were responsible for the theft of more than $6 billion from Binance. The bureau urged service providers that could encounter the stolen funds to improve their controls and watch for related activity. Its statement is an official US law-enforcement attribution; it is not a public court judgment against particular named individuals. The dated original disclosure is the starting point for the figures and product scope discussed here.

Large cryptocurrency thefts seldom finish when tokens leave the initial wallet. Attackers can route funds through liquidity pools, bridges, exchanges or other infrastructure in attempts to change their traceable form. That creates a difficult job for compliance teams: they must examine transaction provenance without assuming that every downstream address has the same relationship to an alleged crime.

The case pushes operational risk beyond the exchange that suffered the original incident. Wallet services, market makers and bridge operators need procedures for receiving reliable alerts, reviewing exposure and acting consistently with local laws. A blockchain transaction can settle quickly, while legal review and asset recovery can require coordination across jurisdictions and institutions.

A crypto incident produces several separate timelines: containment, investigation, recovery and a return to reliable service. Those phases can overlap without finishing together. Keeping those differences visible helps readers judge progress more accurately.

It also does not mean every blockchain network, user or service touched by funds has knowingly facilitated laundering. Security announcements should identify the system affected, the customers who can act, the evidence supporting any recovery number and what remains uncertain. Clear scope protects users from false reassurance and from unnecessary panic. It also provides a sensible basis for follow-up accountability.

Future developments worth following include additional wallet identifiers, court filings, asset seizures or coordinated law-enforcement requests. Platforms will also need to explain how they distinguish high-confidence linked addresses from unrelated users. The outcome matters to customers because indiscriminate blocking can create its own access and due-process problems even when the original security threat is serious.

#5217. Hyperliquid (HYPE) Rallies as CFTC Approves First-Ever US Stock Perpetuals

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

Hyperliquid’s native token (HYPE) responded with confident growth to the historic decision of the U.S. Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC), which for the first time allowed trading of perpetual futures on individual stocks (Equity Perpetuals).

The regulatory shift de facto legitimizes a product that developed predominantly in DeFi ecosystems and opens the way for the institutional expansion of Hyperliquid’s infrastructure, which served as the trigger for the asset’s rally.

According to the CFTC release of November 3, 2026, equity perpetual contracts received the status of security futures under joint CFTC and SEC oversight, with the possibility of trading on dual-registered exchanges.

Today, equity perps can begin trading for the first time on @CFTC registered exchanges. This move reflects my commitment to ensuring that America’s markets remain the most innovative, liquid, and well-regulated in the world. Instead of being forced to turn to offshore, unregulated venues to access equity perpetual futures, Americans may now access equity perps on CFTC-regulated markets subject to robust safeguards. I appreciate the continued partnership of @SECGov and @SECPaulSAtkins as we work to further harmonize our agencies' regulatory initiatives.

The agency’s goal is to bring back into the U.S. legal field billions in volume that had been flowing to offshore crypto platforms. The instrument is being launched under the Commodity Futures Modernization Act of 2000 (CFMA), which eliminates the need to pass new laws.

The regulator’s pragmatic step directly boosts Hyperliquid’s capitalization. Through its HIP-3 framework (in particular, the trade[XYZ] platform), the project already controls the largest on-chain market for perps on Apple, Nvidia and Tesla, with daily turnover in the hundreds of millions of dollars.

The new CFTC classification fully matched the structure that the Hyperliquid Policy Center advocated in official submissions to the authorities.

Now financial giant ICE (owner of the NYSE), which previously invested in trade[XYZ], gets a legal basis for creating a regulated American platform on Hyperliquid technology, while Payward (Kraken’s parent company) is accelerating the launch of a similar product through the regulated exchange Bitnomial.

Despite legalization in the U.S., Hyperliquid maintains a strict geoblock for American residents, continuing to serve the global market thanks to its key trump cards: on-chain liquidity, 24/7 trading and flexible margin conditions.

Local exchanges in the U.S. will inevitably face the limitations of traditional Wall Street sessions and strict SEC leverage limits in the early stages.

This moves Hyperliquid from the status of an offshore experiment into the role of the main technology donor of the American financial market, with the possibility of monetizing its infrastructure through fee opportunities from the trades of regulated partners.

The market instantly priced these prospects into the asset’s value. TradingView charts recorded a vertical surge in the HYPE/USD pair immediately after the release was published at 14:02 UTC: the price impulsively broke out of the $84.9-$85.30 corridor tested a local high above $87.40 and consolidated around $86.83, holding a net gain of more than 2%.

Plasma [XPL] continued its downtrend, dropping 10.22% over 24 hours, with new discoveries indicating potential connections to earlier liquidations.

At the time of reporting, the altcoin price sat around $0.06335, whereas its spot trading volume decreased by 22.35% to $102.48 million.

The sell-off followed a November 2 funding trace that identified possible connections between November liquidation addresses and April traders.

Investigators reportedly traced the funds through an exchange hot wallet before USDT withdrawals reached addresses associated with November trading.

But the results were not sufficient to prove the common ownership or control of the wallets.

Earlier, on April 3, XPL surged 42% to $0.167 before plunging over 40% within minutes.

Five addresses opened long positions prior to the rally, sold margin during the climb and ended up being liquidated.

Meanwhile, during the initial surge, about $32.99 million in short positions were liquidated.

The suspected connections renewed concerns about leveraged trading activity and its potential impact on XPL.

XPL derivatives traders significantly reduced their exposure on the latest sell-off, amidst the growing uncertainty in leveraged markets.

Open Interest fell 15.82% to $171.42 million, showing a significant decrease in outstanding positions, according to CoinGlass data.

Likewise, volume in derivatives trading decreased by 8.78% to $203.62 million, reflecting lower activity in futures markets.

The decline in derivatives activity indicated increasing withdrawal from leveraged exposure by traders after the recent volatility.

Such deleveraging may temporarily decrease liquidation risks, but continued selling pressure could still undernine any stabilization attempts.

The metric has generally been positive throughout October but dipped into the negative territory during the recent drop.

The negative reading meant the shorts were paying longs, suggesting a bearish imbalance in perpetual futures.

Notably, earlier positive funding increases had indicated higher demand for long leveraged positions prior to a decline in sentiment.

However, the latest negative reading came with declining Open Interest, which indicated that traders were reducing their positions as uncertainty increased.

The funding reversal confirmed bearish pressure even though the magnitude of the funding rate was not enough to create significant short pressure.

More importantly, the token’s Relative Strength Index (RSI) dropped to 34.07, below its moving average of 40.79.

It is important to note that even though the indicator approached oversold territory, the bulls were yet to demonstrate a convincing price reversal.

For now, sellers retained control, leaving the $0.0600 support as the decisive level for XPL’s next move.

Besides, buyers would need stronger demand to prevent another decline and establish a credible recovery around this key support.

#5215. $8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

$8 Million Crypto Kidnapping Lands Texas Brothers 15-Year Prison Terms

Two Texas brothers have been sentenced to a combined 326 months in prison for a violent home-invasion robbery in which they held a Minnesota family at gunpoint for hours and forced the father to hand over more than $8 million in cryptocurrency.

Raymond Christian Garcia and Isiah Angelo Garcia, both of Waller, Texas, were sentenced on Thursday to 180 months and 186 months respectively, or 15 and 15-and-a-half years, by U.S. District Judge Donovan Frank in St. Paul, according to the Justice Department. Both had pleaded guilty in June.

According to court documents, the brothers traveled from Texas to Grant, Minnesota, and on Sept. 19, 2025, broke into the victim’s home, zip-tied the family and held them at gunpoint for more than nine hours while demanding access to the father’s crypto accounts.

When he failed to transfer the amount they expected, they forced him to drive one of the brothers to the family’s cabin in upstate Minnesota to retrieve additional hardware wallets and move the funds. In total, they compelled him to transfer over $8 million. The pair fled after the victim’s son called 911, and were later arrested near Houston.

“The Garcia brothers committed a brazen and calculated act of violence against the victim and his family,” U.S. Attorney Daniel Rosen said in a statement. The FBI’s Christopher Dotson said the brothers “violated the sanctity of a home” in “a brazen act of violence and greed.”

The case is a stark example of a “wrench attack,” in which criminals use physical force rather than hacking to seize crypto.

Such attacks have surged alongside crypto’s rising prices: Chainalysis counted 46 violent incidents in the first half of 2026 with more than $30 million stolen, while CertiK pegged losses even higher. Recent cases span a French kidnapping in which workers’ children were held hostage and a UK home invasion that ended in prison terms for the attackers.

The sentencing offers one of the clearest signals yet that U.S. courts are treating crypto wrench attacks as serious violent crime.

#5214. 99% Cold Wallet Rule Mulled by South Korea Following Binance Hack

South Korean financial regulators are reportedly considering introducing much stricter cryptocurrency storage requirements for local exchanges following the massive $6.3 billion Binance hack.

According to a Nov. 3 report by South Korean news outlet News1, the country’s Financial Services Commission (FSC) is discussing raising the minimum proportion of customer cryptocurrencies that exchanges must store in cold wallets from the current 80% to approximately 99%.

The potential regulatory change comes just days after Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange, suffered one of the biggest security breaches in cryptocurrency history.

However, the proposed 99% threshold remains under discussion, and no official decision has been made.

Under South Korea’s existing Virtual Asset User Protection Act, cryptocurrency exchanges are required to store at least 80% of customers’ digital assets in cold wallets.

Unlike hot wallets, which are connected to the internet and used to process everyday transactions, cold wallets are kept offline, making them significantly less vulnerable to remote hacking attacks.

However, the proportion of assets stored offline varies significantly among South Korea’s leading cryptocurrency exchanges.

According to Financial Supervisory Service data cited by local media, Upbit currently has one of the highest cold wallet storage ratios in the country.

As of the end of September, the exchange held approximately 99.17% of customer assets in cold wallets.

Meanwhile, rival cryptocurrency exchange Bithumb stored approximately 94.76% of its assets offline, while Coinone’s cold wallet ratio stood at 85%.

#5212. Ex-XRP Treasury Reveals Exit From $5 Million Stake in Ripple

Disclaimer: The opinions expressed by our writers are their own and do not represent the views of U.Today. The financial and market information provided on U.Today is intended for informational purposes only. U.Today is not liable for any financial losses incurred while trading cryptocurrencies. Conduct your own research by contacting financial experts before making any investment decisions. We believe that all content is accurate as of the date of publication, but certain offers mentioned may no longer be available.

A fresh annual report of British company VivoPower PLC, filed with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) on November 2, 2026, officially disclosed the parameters of the closure of its cryptocurrency strategy.

The documents recorded the creation and subsequent liquidation of a $5 million stake in the private company Ripple Labs, while also making public the price of their buyback at $300 apiece.

According to the filing, in May 2025, VivoPower announced a large-scale treasury expansion with a target investment volume of up to $100 million in XRP tokens and Ripple Labs equity. In practice, the investments turned out to be more modest: the XRP treasury allocated $5.0 million to the direct acquisition of 41,810 Ripple Labs shares in January 2026.

For institutional investors, buying stakes in the fintech giant itself was a key way to make a large bet on the ecosystem's success.

However, the experiment lasted only nine months from its announcement. As early as February, VivoPower's board of directors decided to fully wind down the crypto direction. Management recognized holding digital assets as less profitable than developing artificial intelligence infrastructure and redirected capital to the purchase of a 41.5 MW data center in Mo i Rana, Norway, for $37.7 million.

The process of liquidating this $5 million stake brought the crypto market an important price insight. In April 2026, Ripple Labs conducted a targeted buyback, taking 882 shares from VivoPower at $300 apiece. The deal brought the British company $264,600 in cash and $159,122 in net profit, giving the community a rare and hard marker of how Ripple's management itself values its business in private trades.

A direct sale of the remaining part of the stake on the open market proved impossible due to the non-public status of the securities. As a result, VivoPower entered into agreements to sell the economic rights to the shares to two South Korean partners, KWeather Co. and Lean Ventures.

As of the filing date, the Korean deals have not yet been legally completed. The remaining 40,928 shares are locked in the Korea Securities Depository (KSD) under a strict one-year lock-up restriction. Due to this freeze and the lack of market liquidity, independent appraisers lowered the book value of this stake to $4.3 million (about $106.16 per share).

The VivoPower story clearly showed the crypto industry the flip side of institutional investments: a direct purchase of stakes in Ripple Labs comes with strict lock-ups and low liquidity, where the only fast source of fiat for exiting the ecosystem is the issuer itself through a private buyback procedure.

#5211. Binance Reopens Withdrawals as Hack Recovery Enters Second Week

Binance has reopened withdrawals after its $6.3 billion North Korea hack, covered 95% of losses, and has reserves for the remaining 5%, with post-mortem reviews to stay open.

Binance restarted customer withdrawals on Sunday, four days after hackers drained roughly $6.3 billion from its hot wallets in the largest crypto theft on record.

The exchange reopened in stages over the weekend, starting with bitcoin and expanding to 15 networks. The move marked the first real test of whether the platform could absorb a loss of that size without freezing its customers out for longer.

The recovery push began Saturday. In its third recovery update, Binance said it had replenished 90% of the impacted amount with company funds. The exchange drew on part of its Secure Asset Fund for Users, a portion of its futures insurance fund and its corporate treasury to buy replacement tokens.

The futures fund contribution drew particular attention, since Binance described the remaining balance as a regulatory requirement. Binance also published a new proof-of-reserves snapshot dated October 31, giving users a baseline to check their balances against.

Sunday’s reopening began at 06:00 UTC with BTC. Binance then added ETH, BNB Smart Chain, Solana, XRP, Dogecoin, Tron, Polygon, Avalanche, Arbitrum, Base, Optimism, Zcash, TON and Sui. Between 06:00 and 15:30 UTC, the exchange processed about $4.65 billion in withdrawals against roughly $1.01 billion in deposits.

That produced a net outflow near $3.64 billion, equal to about 2.1% of the $171 billion in assets Binance reported in its late-October proof of reserves.

Deposits stayed open throughout the rollout, and the exchange said temporary delays were possible during the initial surge in volume.

Binance said its Sunday update raised coverage to 95% of the impacted amount. The remaining 5% sits in less liquid tokens outside the top ten assets on its proof-of-reserves list. Sourcing those tokens at the required scale takes more time, and the exchange said it is working with industry partners and over-the-counter desks to complete the purchases. Binance has committed to covering the full amount, and it has repeated that users will not bear the financial impact of the theft.

On Monday, Binance said the full zero-knowledge proof for the October 31 snapshot is complete. Users holding BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD or ZEC can run the exchange’s verifier to confirm their balances are backed at least 1:1.

The verifier is open source, and the exchange has pointed users to a step-by-step guide.

The exchange plans another snapshot covering more tokens on November 4, with a proof expected to take 40 to 50 hours to generate. A further withdrawal phase is scheduled for Tuesday, and the exchange said it would share details once the rollout is ready.

On the law enforcement front, the FBI has attributed the attack to North Korea, and the U.S. Secret Service is working with Circle to trace and freeze funds. Tether reported about $1.57 billion in USDT frozen, and Circle said it had frozen more than $800 million in USDC in coordination with authorities.

The market reacted sharply to the breach. Bitcoin fell about 5% to below $77,000 in the hours after the attack, and shares of Coinbase,Robinhood, Strategy, and Strive all declined as investors weighed the risk of exchange-level losses across the sector.

Binance has said it found additional points of breach beyond the initial compromise, which it originally attributed to a single engineer’s laptop. The exchange has not yet published a full post-mortem explaining how the attackers prepared and executed the operation, which some estimates suggest took at least 36 hours of groundwork.

The destination of roughly $3.3 billion in stolen funds that were not frozen has also not been fully explained, and the exchange has not detailed what it expects to recover from that pool. Investigators continue to track wallet activity across ten blockchains.

#5210. Tesla, Amazon Deal Fresh Blow to OKX Tokenization Push

OKX has suffered another big blow to its grand aspirations of tokenizing stocks after Tesla, Amazon, and four other big names turned down plans for the tokenization of their shares on the cryptocurrency exchange’s joint venture with New York Stock Exchange owner Intercontinental Exchange (ICE).

Six new companies have raised objections to their shares being tokenized on the OKXICE platform.

Among the new objectors are Elon Musk’s Tesla and SpaceX, e-commerce giant Amazon, streaming giant Netflix, semiconductor equipment maker Lam Research and fintech firm SoFi Technologies.

The new objections bring the total number of companies that have objected to the initiative to 56, further damaging OKX’s push into the realm of traditional finance.

In particular, five of the Magnificent Seven tech companies have turned down the proposal to have their stocks tokenized on the OKXICE platform. These include Tesla, Amazon, Microsoft, Alphabet and Nvidia.

What makes this rejection even more noteworthy is that the total number of targeted companies involved in this OKXICE joint venture is relatively low.

Back in early October, OKX and ICE announced their plan to offer tokenized versions of 62 major companies’ stocks.

However, 49 out of those 62 companies have formally opposed the proposal. Hence, about 79% of the initially targeted companies have rejected the plan. The other seven objections came from companies not in the initial selection.

Some of the most famous names in American finance and tech, including JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Walmart, Coca-Cola, Intel, Oracle, AMD, Nike and Costco, have also expressed their objections.

As of the Nov. 2 notice, only 13 out of the original selection had yet to raise a publicly disclosed objection.

These names include Apple, Coinbase, Robinhood, Strategy, Circle and Palantir.

However, the fact that they have not raised a formal objection doesn’t necessarily mean they approve of the tokenization plan.

It is worth noting that Nov. 3 marks the end of the 30-day objection period for the companies that received their initial notice on Oct. 4.

Nonetheless, with almost four-fifths of the initially targeted companies raising objections, OKXICE faces an uphill battle to get sufficient corporate involvement to make its tokenized stock market appealing to investors.

OKXICE has not set a definite launch date yet. It also has not disclosed the number of tokenized stocks it will offer when it starts trading.

#5209. Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Binance Hack: Why Bitcoin Didn't Crash Like It Did in 2018

Bitcoin is down about 4% a day after the biggest crypto hack on record. In March 2018, a failed attack on the same exchange knocked nearly 11% off its price in minutes, and Binance said at the time that no funds had been stolen.

Binance has confirmed yesterday's attack, and Chainalysis and TRM Labs put the theft at roughly $6.4 billion, more than four times the previous record hack: the $1.5 billion Bybit heist of February 2025. Bitcoin dipped to about $76,400 on Friday, around 6% below where it traded after Wednesday’s Federal Reserve decision.

More than $1.3 billion in leveraged bets was liquidated, meaning trades placed with borrowed money that exchanges close automatically when prices move against them. And yet Bitcoin is holding relatively steady, all things considered.

The 2018 incident was a phishing and theft attempt: attackers took over accounts, created API keys (credentials that let trading bots act on an account) and used them to pump the obscure coin Viacoin while 31 accounts that had pre-deposited the coin sold it. Bitcoin fell from $10,740 to $9,690 within minutes and kept dropping to about $6,600 by April 5, though the market was already in a bear run after peaking near $20,000 in December 2017.

To have a grasp of the situation, the entire crypto market was worth just over $400 billion in March 2018 and is worth roughly $2.6 trillion now.

A big reason for that, and why panic doesn't hit markets the same way anymore, is U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs. Funds like BlackRock’s iShares Bitcoin Trust let people buy Bitcoin through an ordinary brokerage account, and they now hold roughly 1.3 million BTC, about 6% of the supply.

Their shareholders don’t keep coins on Binance, and sovereign wealth funds such as Mubadala and the Abu Dhabi Investment Council have been buying. BlackRock’s fund alone took in $136 million on Wednesday, per Farside Investors data.

These funds don’t tend to panic sell so easily, and those funds remain locked for long periods of time.

The theft began at 4:05 p.m. ET Thursday, after U.S. markets closed, so Thursday’s preliminary $17 million of ETF inflows predate it and Friday’s flow figures are the first real read on whether those holders are selling.

The attacker still holds more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, about $2.4 billion that no stablecoin issuer can freeze. The amount doesn’t account for other tokens, which would increase the holdings to more than $3 billion.

TRM and Chainalysis tie the attack to the North Korean group behind Bybit, which after that heist converted about 84% of the stolen Ethereum into Bitcoin through Thorchain, a cross-chain swap network.

Binance says it has set aside more than $6.34 billion of company funds to cover every loss, and it is buying the missing tokens on the open market and over the counter, meaning in private deals off the exchange. About $3 billion is frozen, deposits are operating normally, and withdrawals are on track to resume in 45 hours.

Market sentiment was already soft before the theft and slid further: CoinMarketCap’s Fear and Greed index, a 0-to-100 gauge of market mood, dropped to 36 points from 43 (now in “fear” territory after being “neutral” yesterday), and Bitcoin sits about 38% below its $126,080 record. Back in 2018, the market was well in “extreme fear” territory at 12 points on the scale when the incident happened. Traders were already in panic mode, and the attack just worsened an already ugly situation.

Glassnode data show long-term holders underwater on about 4 million coins bought between $85,000 and $118,000, and HashKey analyst Tim Sun said before the hack that the market’s problem “is not panic selling, but rather insufficient new buying interest.”

So, taken all together, what it all boils down to is this: A more optimistic market, much more liquidity, active long-term investors, a clearer regulatory framework, a sense of safety thanks to that regulatory clarity, quick action by stablecoin providers, and a price trend in recovery likely all played a role in keeping Bitcoin safe from a global panic despite the record-breaking hack.

#5208. Brazil’s Mercado Bitcoin Freezes Deposits and Withdrawals Due to Ongoing Security Incident, Losses Estimated Near $40 Million

Brazil’s largest crypto exchange Mercado Bitcoin says customers suffered no losses, but it has not said how much was taken or when it will reopen.

Brazil’s largest cryptocurrency exchange kept deposits and withdrawals suspended on Monday after a security incident in one of its systems. The shutdown left customers unable to send funds into or out of the platform while the company worked to restore operations.

Mercado Bitcoin said in a statement posted to X that it had identified and contained the incident. The company said customers suffered no losses and that no data had leaked. It described the suspension as a precaution. Its teams are investigating, tracing assets and coordinating with authorities, the company said. According to Bloomberg, the company said it would absorb all losses.

The company has not said which system was breached, how much was taken or when it will reopen. Its current statements gave no total loss figure and no timetable.

Mercado Bitcoin has since published destination addresses tied to transactions from the incident, covering Bitcoin, Ethereum-compatible networks, Solana, Stellar and Tron. It said it shared the addresses for identification and tracking purposes. Blockchain security firm Hypernative has put preliminary estimates of suspicious transfers above $40 million across several blockchains. The exchange has not confirmed that figure.

Meanwhile, Ether traded near $2,290 on November 3, as market participants watched the $2,300 level for signs of a sustained recovery.

Lookonchain reported on X today that BitMine Immersion Technologies, led by Fundstrat’s Tom Lee, bought 5,000 ETH.

The company is now roughly 99% of the way toward its stated target of controlling 5% of all circulating ETH.

Crypto analyst Ted Pillows noted on X that Ethereum has been holding up well over the past few days, suggesting that buyers are gradually stepping in.

According to his latest analysis, ETH needs to reclaim the $2,300 level to establish the foundation for a stronger recovery rally.

Now is the time for $ETH to start its reversal.In the past few days, Ethereum has definitely been holding up well, which indicates buyers are stepping in.Now, ETH needs to reclaim $2,300 for a decent rally. pic.twitter.com/6WgkgbUc0T

The focus is now on whether Ethereum can maintain its recovered support and build enough momentum to break through the next resistance levels.

Ether slipped 0.2% on Tuesday, according to CoinGecko data. It is down 1.4% over the past week and 15.6% over the past month.

On-chain analytics from Santiment Intelligence reveal a contraction in liquid ETH supply across centralized exchanges following the October 29 historical $6.39 billion hack at Binance.

According to Santiment post on X, ETH exchange supply declined from 3.56% of total supply on October 28 to 3.20% by November 2. Over half of the initial drop occurred immediately during the hot-wallet incident, draining ~200,630 ETH.

Corporate treasuries like BitMine are absorbing a growing share of ETH supply while exchange balances shrink, which can reduce liquid supply available to sellers.

#5206. Tether freezes $1.5 billion in 30 minutes after Binance hack, but $729 million escapes

Tether says its response kept stolen USDT out of North Korea's reach, but investigators found attackers had already converted hundreds of millions into other cryptocurrencies.

Tether froze more than $1.5 billion in stolen USDT within 30 minutes of Binance's Oct. 29 hack, highlighting stablecoins' ability to disrupt cryptocurrency theft.

In a Nov. 2 statement, the world's largest stablecoin issuer said its intervention prevented the immobilized funds from being transferred or laundered by attackers linked to North Korea.

The announcement followed an Oct. 31 FBI alert attributing the theft of more than $6 billion from Binance to North Korea's TraderTraitor hacking operation, which US authorities have linked to efforts to finance the country's weapons programs.

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said the incident demonstrated why USDT was an unattractive vehicle for criminals, arguing that the company's ability to rapidly block suspicious addresses made it difficult for attackers to retain access to stolen assets.

The intervention also prompted a broader debate about stablecoins' effectiveness in combating financial crime.

Rayne Steinberg, co-founder of digital asset investment firm Arca, contrasted Tether's response with traditional banks' histories of processing illicit transactions, arguing that financial institutions have often identified money laundering only after funds had already moved through their systems.

Steinberg pointed to major banking scandals involving sanctions evasion and criminal proceeds, questioning the criticism frequently directed at stablecoins over their potential use in illicit finance.

“Turns out a ledger you can actually see beats a duffel bag you can't.”

Tether cited its broader enforcement record to support that argument. The company said it has frozen more than $7 billion in USDT across secondary-market addresses, including the latest Binance intervention, and helped return approximately $1.2 billion to rightful owners and law enforcement through token reissuance.

It also reported working with more than 360 law enforcement agencies across 73 countries to investigate financial crime and restrict suspicious transactions.

Despite the rapid intervention, blockchain investigators found that attackers managed to move substantial amounts of stolen USDT before Tether could restrict their wallets.
