The exchange said the remaining assets were less liquid and took longer to source in the required quantities. It described continued purchases through market and over-the-counter channels, alongside work with industry partners to finish the acquisitions.

In its Oct. 31 update, Binance identified corporate treasury, a planned partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users and a portion of its futures insurance resources as recovery funding sources.

The percentage also has a narrower meaning than a general recovery score. Binance describes the denominator as the impacted amount. It does not specify the valuation basis in those disclosures, so the remaining 5% cannot safely be converted into a dollar shortfall. Nor does 95% describe the proportion of tokens or withdrawal routes restored.

Binance's Nov. 2 update said the zero-knowledge proof for its Oct. 31, 00:00 UTC reserve snapshot was complete. It said holders of the ten named tokens could independently verify that their balances were backed at least one-for-one.

The proof concerns a defined set of assets at a dated snapshot. Backing at that snapshot does not establish present availability of every withdrawal route.

The latest withdrawal table illustrates why the network matters alongside the token. It includes RLUSD on Ethereum and XRP, and USDC on Sui, among routes planned for progressive reopening. Those entries identify the scope of the rollout, not which tokens remain unreplenished or whether each route is currently operational.

Binance said it was prioritizing tokens according to demand over the preceding week and restoring each network-asset pair after security checks.

For holders, the immediate check is the chosen asset and network. Binance directed users to its withdrawal page or network-status page to confirm availability, and warned that rollout traffic could cause temporary delays or longer processing times.

A separate transparency milestone is still ahead. Binance said it planned a Nov. 4 reserve snapshot covering more tokens, followed by proof generation estimated to take approximately 40 to 50 hours.

Binance reiterated that it would bear the financial impact of the stolen funds rather than pass losses on to users.

#5171. Binance Hack: Everything You Need to Know About the Biggest Crypto Heist in History

Binance Hack: Everything You Need to Know About the Biggest Crypto Heist in History

Crypto just suffered the largest hack in its history.

On Thursday, attackers drained roughly $6.4 billion from Binance, the world’s largest cryptocurrency exchange, in a breach that dwarfs every theft before it and has triggered a cross-border law-enforcement investigation, a scramble by stablecoin issuers to freeze funds, and a tense wait for millions of users.

Here’s what’s known about the Binance hack so far.

The theft began at about 4:05 p.m. ET on Thursday, after U.S. markets had closed, when attackers moved funds out of Binance hot wallets across roughly 10 blockchains, most of it within about 20 minutes.

Blockchain analytics firms Chainalysis and TRM Labs put the total at around $6.4 billion, more than four times the previous record, February 2025’s $1.5 billion Bybit heist.

Crucially, the attack targeted Binance’s own infrastructure, not the blockchains themselves. Binance initially traced the breach to a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer, which may have let the attacker reach a service container and submit unauthorized withdrawals.

The exchange has since said its investigation uncovered “additional points of breach,” with CEO Richard Teng describing “a more complex attack process” that “did not simply use one engineer’s permissions.” Binance said it found no evidence that wallet private keys were leaked, and that the attacker used a self-destruct mechanism to wipe tooling and traces along the way.

On Ethereum, the attacker leaned on EIP-7702, a feature introduced in 2025 that lets ordinary wallets temporarily run smart-contract code.

Using 50 pre-signed authorizations, the attacker turned a Binance hot wallet into a “sweeper” contract and drained its assets, according to TRM Labs.

Teng said that delegation “did not pass through our normal approval controls.” The episode didn’t expose a flaw in Ethereum itself, but rather, TRM said, pointed to a compromise of Binance’s signing infrastructure.

TRM Labs and Chainalysis both attributed the hack to TraderTraitor, a North Korean state-linked group also blamed for the Bybit heist.

After that attack, the group converted roughly 84% of the stolen Ethereum into Bitcoin via the cross-chain network Thorchain. The attribution pushes North Korea’s 2026 crypto haul well past $1 billion, and the Binance figure alone would dwarf the rest.

Teng said Binance will cover every dollar of the losses with company funds and its Secure Asset Fund for Users, an emergency reserve, stressing that customer assets were never touched.

The exchange has set aside more than $6.34 billion, about $5 billion of it already held in the exact tokens stolen, including BTC, ETH, BNB, USDC, and USDT, and is buying the rest on the open market and over the counter.

Binance paused withdrawals to rebuild and retest its systems, is running a full security review, and aims to restore withdrawals by Sunday while publishing a complete proof of reserves by Monday. Deposits are operating normally.

Stablecoin issuers moved fast following the initial reports of a suspected attack, unlike previous security incidents.

Stablecoins are the backbone of the crypto economy—dollar-pegged tokens designed to hold a steady value that enable quick and easy transactions on both centralized and decentralized exchanges. Unlike traditional digital currencies, like Bitcoin or Ethereum, stablecoins are centralized, issued and managed by private companies. That means these funds can be frozen at any time, and for any reason.

Tether, the company behind the biggest stablecoin on the market USDT, froze more than $1.5 billion in assets. Circle, which issues the second largest stablecoin USDC, froze around $823 million tied to the attack, both using their ability to blacklist specific addresses.

In all, roughly $3 billion has been frozen or located. But a large sum remains beyond reach: the attacker still controls more than 420,000 ETH and 18,270 BTC, plus other tokens, worth over $3 billion that no issuer can freeze, and has kept shuffling funds through Tron and other networks. Security firm Elliptic estimated the hacker still controls around $3.3 billion.

Binance is cooperating with law enforcement worldwide, including the FBI and the U.S. Secret Service.

The FBI confirmed it was aware of the incident and coordinating with partners; the Secret Service, which investigates financial and digital-currency crimes, declined to comment.

It echoes earlier joint efforts like April’s Operation Atlantic, which traced tens of millions in stolen crypto.

For a theft of this scale, markets stayed remarkably composed.

Bitcoin fell to about $76,400 on Friday, down roughly 4%, and more than $1.3 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated.

That’s a far cry from March 2018, when a failed attack on Binance knocked nearly 11% off Bitcoin in minutes. Analysts credit a bigger, more mature market, now worth around $2.6 trillion, with spot Bitcoin ETFs holding roughly 1.3 million BTC in hands that don’t panic-sell.

Wall Street firms including Cantor Fitzgerald, TD Cowen, and Compass Point even urged clients to buy crypto-linked stocks on the weakness, framing the hack as a Binance security failure rather than a threat to the underlying technology.

CFTC Chairman Michael Selig seized on the breach to argue offshore exchanges need U.S. oversight. Selig said in an interview with CNBC that the breach “proves our exact point” behind the crypto-market regulations the agency proposed earlier this month.

And the episode sparked a public spat between Binance founder Changpeng Zhao, better known as CZ, and Elon Musk. The SpaceX and Tesla founder appeared to troll Binance over the hack on X, and CZ fired back, citing Musk’s own SpaceX stake. "Our SPCX shares (from our $500m investment in your Twitter takeover) are worth more than a billion. Would appreciate if you show a little support," CZ posted.

CZ struck a defiant note overall: the theft is the largest in dollar terms, he said, but not in Bitcoin terms, and unlike Mt. Gox or FTX, Binance is absorbing the loss itself.

#5169. Binance Says $6.34 Billion Hack Began With Fake Job Interview

Binance’s incident report says the $6.34 billion Oct. 29 theft was a five-month campaign that began with a fake job interview and a poisoned developer tool.

The $6.34 billion theft from Binance began with a fake job interview and ran for five months before a single coin moved, the exchange said Tuesday in its first public account of the Oct. 29 attack.

Another initial incident report from Binance, released alongside a media briefing by co-CEO Richard Teng and chief security officer Jimmy Su, describes a campaign that combined a poisoned software download, a staged recruitment process, malware that evaded endpoint detection and an internal AI tool the attackers turned against the company’s own wallet engineers.

Binance said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion of the stolen assets and replenished 98% of affected tokens from its own funds, with the balance being bought on the open market.

“This wasn’t one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign,” Teng wrote on X. “We’re publishing the full report because the lessons go far beyond Binance.”

The FBI has attributed the attack to North Korea. Binance’s report cites that finding but stops short of its own attribution. Pyongyang rejected the claim Monday, with Kim Yo Jong, sister of leader Kim Jong Un, calling it a “grave political provocation” through state media.

According to Binance, the first foothold for this hack came in June. A wallet DevOps engineer searched for a popular AI coding tool and downloaded a tampered Homebrew installer.

Binance’s security team caught the anomaly, replaced the device and rotated credentials, but the episode exposed the engineer’s role and the names of colleagues on the wallet infrastructure team.

In September, a second engineer was approached on a legitimate job platform by someone posing as a project director. A video interview with a purported HR representative followed, with the interviewer sharing a personal social media account to build trust. A second interview with a supposed CEO ended with an “assignment”: a code repository the engineer was told to install on his work machine.

The repository replaced a legitimate pyenv component with a malicious version and opened a channel to an attacker-controlled server. Binance said the zero-day malware was not flagged by its endpoint tools at the time.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used that machine and an internal AI tool to reach two more wallet engineers, one of whom held a container deployment token. On Oct. 29, between 17:15 and 19:56 UTC, they logged into a third engineer’s computer, deployed a container with the stolen credentials and launched the drain. Binance said it blocked 57 other suspected North Korea-linked attempts this year using similar lures.

The money left fast. Between 20:05 and 20:09 UTC, five hot wallets sent 233.46 million XRP to an attacker address. Wallets on Solana and Optimism followed with about 970,500 SOL and 44.2 million OP. A Binance hot wallet then sent 18,270 BTC.

The attacker used the compromised signing service to invoke Ethereum’s EIP-7702, which lets an account delegate execution rights to a smart contract without exposing its key, and gave an attacker-controlled contract partial control of a Binance hot wallet.

Suspending withdrawals did not stop that last channel. The attackers had submitted 50 signing transactions to establish on-chain delegations that operated outside Binance’s withdrawal infrastructure. Because the wallet used multi-party computation, no single key existed to revoke them, and the team had to build a procedure on the spot. The final unauthorized transfer, 2,961.9 ETH, settled at 23:56 UTC.

Binance cleared the delegation at 00:10 UTC on Oct. 30.

The report acknowledges the gap. “These challenges extended the time required to fully contain the unauthorized asset movements,” it says, adding that the company is reviewing its signing policy coverage and hot wallet controls.

Customers withdrew $10.97 billion in the 46 hours after withdrawals reopened Sunday, with net outflows of $5.28 billion, according todata Teng shared Tuesday. By 03:00 UTC hourly deposits exceeded withdrawals, a shift Teng called a sign of stabilization. Binance reopened withdrawals for dozens more assets across nine networks on Tuesday.

The breach has become a talking point in Washington. CFTC Chairman Michael Selig said the hack “proves our exact point” about the need for a federal registration path for offshore exchanges, and on Tuesday the agency cleared equity perpetual futures for U.S.-regulated venues, extending the onshoring push it began with Kalshi’s bitcoin perp in May.

Binance said it has rebuilt the wallet system and the internal AI tool in new environments, replaced affected devices, revoked the engineers’ credentials and expanded behavioral anomaly detection.

It is commissioning an independent review, raising spending on endpoint and developer-environment security and planning a research fund on AI security with outside firms. Some technical details, the report says, will stay private.

#5168. Hackers hijack a dozen DeFi websites, all registered with Name.com, and steal about $138,000

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Blockaid said the sites were rigged with Step Drainer, a malware kit that tricks visitors into signing away their funds. Ten of the domains have since been suspended.

Hackers who took over a dozen DeFi websites late Friday and used them to empty visitors' crypto wallets targeted domains registered through the same company, Name.com, according to public registry records reviewed by The Block. The Block has traced about $138,000 in stolen funds so far using onchain data.

Name.com is listed as the registrar for all 12 hijacked sites that were identified by security firm Blockaid, and 10 of them were suspended at the registrar level at 8:32 a.m. ET on Saturday.

X user bluexxix was among the first to notice the shared registrar, posting at 11:51 p.m. ET on Friday that it "seems @namedotcom is compromised" as more affected sites surfaced. Pseudonymous developer banteg reached the same conclusion on Saturday morning, and X user punkquidity later spotted that 10 of the domains had been switched off within seconds of each other.

Each of the 10 suspended domains now carries a "client hold" status, which stops a domain from resolving. They include Pickle Finance, Origami Finance, FluxBeam, Echelon, StonkFun and StakePoint. Flap's and Kintsu's domains were not suspended and are still operational as of publishing time.

X user liquidated0x flagged that Flap's site had seemingly been compromised at 9:10 p.m. ET on Friday, about an hour before the token launchpad warned its users that its flap.sh frontend had been hit by "a DNS hijacking incident." Blockaid warned about the attack on Flap and liquid staking protocol Kintsu at 10:54 p.m. ET, naming the 10 additional hijacked sites soon after.

Name.com's only public response so far came at 12:27 a.m. ET, when it replied to liquidated0x and bluexxix on X, asking them to fill out its abuse form. Name.com’s listed press address did not accept The Block’s request for comment, and its parent company, Identity Digital, did not immediately respond.

Blockaid said the hijacked sites were rigged with Step Drainer, a malware toolkit that tricks visitors into approving transfers out of their wallets once they connect them to a site. Security firm LevelBlue SpiderLabs described Step Drainer in April as a "Malware-as-a-Service (MaaS)" kit, reportedly sold for about $750 for the full source code or $150 for a version that takes a 20% cut of each theft. The firm said the kit claims support for more than 20 blockchain networks.

Onchain data reviewed by The Block shows the attackers routing proceeds to three wallets in fixed 15%, 55% and 30% cuts. On BNB Chain, a Safe wallet flagged by liquidated0x received about 3.1 BNB, worth roughly $2,200, from four wallets between 9:00 and 9:42 p.m. ET on Friday. It split the first three deposits among the three wallets, and about 2.27 BNB was still sitting in the Safe on Saturday morning.

The same three wallets received identically split payments on Ethereum, Robinhood Chain, and Monad, the network Kintsu runs on. On Monad, two of the wallets redeemed Kintsu's staked MON token and withdrew from lending protocol Neverland before converting the proceeds into MON. On Robinhood Chain, one wallet pulled tokenized stocks including NVDA and AAPL out of Morpho and swapped them for ETH.

Together the three wallets held about $138,000 late Saturday morning, mostly MON and Robinhood Chain ETH, and almost all of it arrived after the attack started. That's only what The Block could trace on EVM chains. Three of the hijacked sites, FluxBeam, StonkFun and StakePoint, are built on Solana, and Echelon runs on Aptos, so any losses there would sit in other wallets and aren't counted.

The hijacked sites don't share a DNS provider, the service that tells internet browsers which server a domain points to. Some run on Cloudflare, some on Vercel and some on Name.com's own nameservers. Punkquidity said they couldn't yet tell "whether Name.com itself or a reseller like Vercel did it."

Kintsu and Origami Finance have both publicly blamed cloud infrastructure firm Vercel. "It was something that happened on Vercel's end that was out of our control," Kintsu wrote in a reply on X, adding that Vercel had not yet responded. Origami first warned of "an active attack on Vercel-associated Nameservers," and later said its vaults would stay paused while it waits for "root cause analysis from Vercel / name[.]com." Flap builder Cedric said shortly after midnight that the team was reaching out to both companies.

Vercel picked Name.com as the registrar for its rebuilt domain service last year, Name.com said in a December 2025 press release. Vercel had not commented publicly as of Saturday morning, and both companies' status pages listed all systems as operational. In April, Vercel disclosed a breach of its internal systems that it traced to a compromised third-party AI tool, The Block previously reported.

No affected project has reported a victim count as of yet, though onchain data shows at least four wallets sent funds to the attackers on BNB Chain alone. FluxBeam said it had "no reports of users being affected," and StonkFun, which moved its site to new infrastructure overnight, said it was "not aware of anyone affected." Kintsu, Origami, FluxBeam and StonkFun all said their onchain contracts were untouched.

Crypto front-ends have been hit by DNS hijacks before. Aerodrome and Velodrome were hijacked in November 2025, and Galxe was hit in October 2023, when attackers got into the project's account at registrar Dynadot.

Vercel did not immediately respond to a request for comment. Blockaid's PR agency said the firm was looking into The Block's questions.

The suspensions on the hijacked domains appear to have been lifted by late Saturday morning, registry records show. The domains resolve again and now carry locks that block transfers and, for most of them, other changes.

Name.com has allegedly told at least one affected project that the breach was not on its end. Cloakd, the developer behind FluxBeam, said on X that its representative at Name.com was "taking the stance that it’s not them but the 3rd party issuers."

Updated at 2:00 p.m. ET with details from the lifted suspension and Cloakd's post.

Update, Oct. 31, 3:37 p.m. ET: Name.com said 15 customer domains were affected, more than the 12 Blockaid identified.

"name.com identified unauthorized activity late Friday, October 30, that redirected a number of domains registered through the platform,” a Name.com spokesperson said in a statement to The Block. "name.com has restored all affected domains and is working directly with the name.com account holders. We’re continuing to investigate and have taken steps to secure the accounts involved."

Name.com also said on X that the domains "were improperly redirected late Friday as part of a plan to access visitors’ crypto wallets," and urged customers to check their DNS records and enable two-factor authentication. Vercel said in a security bulletin that 10 of the affected domains were associated with its platform, that Name.com has restored them and that it is assisting in the investigation.

#5167. This Is What Weed Really Did to My Body

How a medical marijuana card works for three real guys.

In November 1996, California became the first state to legalize medical cannabis. (Many) politics and (some) physicians promised us breakthroughs in better health. Thirty years later, how are we really doing? Men’s Health decided to find out in this special report package. Read more here.

THREE REAL GUYS. Three dramatically different mental health conditions. Here’s how a weed card works for them.

THE GUY: TJ, a 47-year-old data insights professional

THE CONDITION: Multiple sclerosis, a chronic autoimmune disease that affects the central nervous system

THE EXPERIENCE: TJ was diagnosed with MS at the age of 25. Doctors prescribed him Rebif, a drug that helped his symptoms but made him depressed. “I was immediately suicidal on that medicine,” he says. Years before medical marijuana was legalized in his state, a friend suggested pot. TJ was desperate, tried weed, and says he felt immediate relief. In 2022, he obtained his medical card. Today, TJ struggles with nerve pain and migraines but says cannabis helps him play volleyball, travel, and stay active. “I don’t know that I would have a productive and happy life if I were not treating myself that way.”

THE GUY: CJ, a 32-year-old postdoctoral researcher of addiction neurobiology

THE CONDITION: Sarcoidosis, an inflammatory disease that causes collections of immune cells, called granulomas, to form in the body. Depending on the location, granulomas can result in fatigue, joint pain, nausea, and swelling. CJ has a rare form located in his brain stem.

THE EXPERIENCE: CJ’s symptoms began in his early 20s, starting with skin issues and leading, eventually, to debilitating nausea. After doctors diagnosed him with sarcoidosis in 2021, he went from using cannabis occasionally to using it daily for nausea control. Through self-experimentation, he learned the combination of cannabinoids that worked the best. Because the ratios were harder to find in OTC products, CJ got his medical card in 2023. “This brought down my overall symptom load a huge amount,” CJ says. Along with an off-label Crohn’s disease medication, medical marijuana has brought him back to 90 percent of his old self, he says.

THE GUY: Jonah, a 41-year-old bookstore manager

THE CONDITION: Generalized anxiety disorder (GAD), a mental health condition that causes excessive fear and worry and interferes with day-to-day functioning.

THE EXPERIENCE: Jonah says he was always labeled “the weird kid.” He ran away at 16 and experienced problems with drugs, alcohol, and homelessness in his early 20s, until his mother convinced him to talk to a therapist, who diagnosed him with anxiety and recommended medical marijuana. After using cannabis for a few weeks, Jonah says, the dark fog that had made him want to act out or isolate himself began to lift. “Conversations with people became a lot easier; decision-making, especially good decisions, became a lot easier.” Sixteen years after that therapy appointment, he’s happily married and life is good, he says.

There’s so much more going on in the world of weed and health. Read more here.

Lauren Vinopal is a writer based in Chicago whose work has been published in GQ, Slate, Mel Magazine, and more.

#5165. Binance sees $3.64 billion in net outflows after reopening withdrawals following record hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The exchange said the outflows equal 2.1% of its assets and that withdrawal volumes have eased after an initial spike. Binance said an updated proof-of-reserves report should be released Monday.

Binance recorded about $3.64 billion in net outflows in the first nine and a half hours after it began restoring withdrawals on Sunday, following Thursday's $6.3 billion hack, the exchange said.

Binance processed $4.65 billion in withdrawals and $1.01 billion in deposits between 1 a.m. and 10:30 a.m. ET, the world's largest crypto exchange said on X. It put the net outflow at 2.1% of its $171 billion in assets under management, based on its latest proof-of-reserves data from Oct. 28.

Withdrawal volumes have declined and are "stabilising as expected" after an initial spike, while deposit volumes "have remained substantial," according to Binance. The exchange began restoring withdrawals in phases at 1 a.m. ET and reopened all 15 networks and assets in its first phase by 7:36 a.m. ET.

Binance is still testing the remaining networks and assets, which include Cardano, Chainlink, Hyperliquid, Litecoin and NEAR, according to its status page. As of 11:22 a.m. ET, the page showed withdrawals suspended for 531 of the 552 coins listed, with 14 others, including bitcoin and ether, partly restored.

Binance saw $4.05 billion in net outflows over 24 hours as of 11:37 a.m. ET, according to DefiLlama, which tracks $131.1 billion in assets across the exchange's wallets. The next 10 largest exchanges by assets that DefiLlama tracks saw about $368 million in combined net inflows over the same period, led by OKX at $316.8 million.

Bitfinex and Gate posted net outflows of $106.3 million and $34.5 million, respectively. DefiLlama does not track Coinbase or Kraken, but bitcoin balances on the two exchanges rose by about 984 BTC and 1,422 BTC over 24 hours, while Binance's fell by about 19,289 BTC, according to CoinGlass.

Attackers drained about $6.3 billion from Binance's hot wallets on Thursday in the largest crypto hack on record, leading the exchange to temporarily suspend withdrawals. The FBI attributed the theft to North Korea's TraderTraitor hacking group on Saturday.

Binance now processes withdrawals from newly generated wallets added to its proof-of-reserves list. It said an updated reserves report, which will be based on an Oct. 31 snapshot and computed with zero-knowledge proofs, is projected to be released on Monday.

Binance co-CEO Richard Teng said on Saturday the exchange had replenished 90% of the stolen assets and would cover the rest. Binance said on Friday it had readied $6.34 billion to cover losses from the hack.

#5163. TD Cowen: Democratic Congress Would Sink CLARITY Act for Two Years

TD Cowen’s Jaret Seiberg says a Democratic sweep of Congress would kill the CLARITY Act for two years, hand Elizabeth Warren the Banking Committee and let Democrats block Trump’s picks for the SEC and CFTC.

A Democratic sweep of Congress would end the CLARITY Act’s chances for the next two years and sour the political climate for crypto, TD Cowen’s Washington analyst Jaret Seiberg wrote to Bitcoin Magazine and clients in an election-day note.

Seiberg wrote that TD Cowen agrees with polls and prediction markets that Democrats are likely to take both chambers, with the caveat that polls taken a month out, which were more mixed, tend to beat those taken on the eve of a vote.

His conclusion for crypto is blunt: “Democrats will likely investigate Trump including his crypto entanglements. We see this as souring the political environment for crypto, which is why we do not expect the Clarity Act to pass over the next two years.”

The market structure bill passed the House 294-134 in July 2025 and failed a Senate cloture vote 49-50 on Sept. 15. If it does not pass before the new Congress convenes on Jan. 3, it dies and must be reintroduced.

Seiberg added that crypto’s influence may shrink further in Congress if Sherrod Brown wins his Ohio Senate race, a contest the industry spent to lose.

The note treats Senate control as the bigger prize. A Democratic majority could block Trump’s nominees and use that power to force him to fill Democratic seats on the FDIC, SEC and CFTC, the three agencies at the center of crypto policy.

It could pressure the White House to drop its effort to remove Lisa Cook from the Federal Reserve board. Trump could answer with acting chiefs, Seiberg wrote, but that risks handing authority to career staff, an outcome “Team Trump” does not want.

Senate control would elevate Sen. Elizabeth Warren to chair the Banking Committee. Seiberg sees room for populist bills such as interest rate caps, limits on bank buybacks and a government credit score, and he thinks Warren and Trump could find common ground on some of them.

Rep. Maxine Waters would take House Financial Services, with a focus on housing and tighter scrutiny of the FHFA.

“That means a focus on housing though we are dubious that she will find GOP support for down payment help and public housing renovations. FHFA scrutiny will increase,” the note read.

For banks, the note says the election changes little: regulators will keep easing capital, liquidity and tiering rules whichever party wins. Divided government does raise the odds of a shutdown or a debt-ceiling standoff, since Trump is expected to reject spending conditions Democrats attach.

Bitcoin traded near $77,500 Tuesday afternoon eastern time, up from a dip to $76,300 in the morning and about $2,000 below where it opened the month. The price has absorbed a record $6.34 billion Binance hack, two days of spot ETF outflows and a Fed that is leaning toward a December hike.

Prediction markets on Kalshi give Democrats a 94% chance at the House and 74% at the Senate, so much of Seiberg’s scenario is in the price.

What is not priced is the lame-duck session. Sen. Angela Alsobrooks, a CLARITY negotiator, has said the bill could pass before January even if a Democratic Congress would not make it a priority after. That said, if it fails during that window, she doubts a Democratic-controlled Congress will make it a priority in the future.

According to various reports, crypto groups have spent more than $200 million on the cycle to avoid the outcome Seiberg now expects. The CFTC, for its part, moved without Congress on Tuesday, clearing equity perpetual futures for U.S. exchanges. If the Senate and Congress flips, that agency-by-agency approach, and who sits on those agencies, becomes the whole game.

#5160. How to Run an AI Model Locally—And Why You Might Want To

How to Run an AI Model Locally—And Why You Might Want To

Open-weight AI models now trail the best closed models by only about four months on average, and a growing number of them run on hardware you probably already own.

"Open-weight" means the company publishes the weights, the files holding everything the model learned, so anyone can download and run them. Two free programs, Ollama and LM Studio, handle the rest, and Gemma 4 downloads in Ollama's library range from about 4.6GB to 20GB.

With open models, you don't need to pay $20 a month for an AI subscription or send your conversations to someone else's servers to get a capable chatbot. This guide explains how to download and run AI models directly on your computer, what kind of hardware you'll need, and how to get started with free tools like Ollama and LM Studio.

Whether you want to save money, keep your conversations private, or experiment with AI models you can customize yourself, setting up a local AI assistant may be easier than you think.

LM Studio's documentation says the app "can operate entirely offline, just make sure to get some model files first," and that documents attached to a chat can be used entirely offline too.

Subscriptions to paid chatbots run about $20 per month, while the software here is free. The cost shifts to hardware you may already have.

Licenses decide what you can do with downloaded weights. DeepSeek's R1 series is under the MIT License, and its model card says the models "support commercial use, allow for any modifications and derivative works," while Google's Gemma 4 and OpenAI's gpt-oss ship under Apache 2.0.

Running models on an open-source license like Apache or MIT means you are free to use, modify, and do whatever you want with a model. This means users are able to access improved and modified versions of a specific model that better fit their needs. Maybe you need a model that is good at medicine, so you don’t care about how good it codes. This level of personalization is only available in customized models.

Models are sized in parameters, the internal numbers adjusted during training, and the download grows with them. The model has to fit in your graphics card's video memory, known as VRAM, or in a Mac's unified memory, which the processor and graphics chip share.

As a general rule, you'll want at least 8GB of VRAM, that compressed smaller will be able to fit. However, serious users should buy a gaming GPU with 24GB of VRAM or more, either by stacking up GPUs or by buying specific AI racks or machines with unified memory like the Mac Mini or the Nvidia DGX Spark.

Quantization shrinks a model by storing each weight with fewer bits. Hugging Face's documentation describes it as representing weights and activations "with low-precision data types like 8-bit integer (int8) instead of the usual 32-bit floating point (float32)," which cuts memory use at the cost of some accuracy.

LM Studio's system requirements recommend 16GB or more of RAM on Apple Silicon Macs running macOS 14 or newer, though 8GB Macs can handle smaller models. On Windows it recommends at least 16GB of RAM and 4GB of dedicated VRAM, with AVX2 processor support, and Intel-based Macs are not supported.

Ollama's library lists Gemma 4 26B at 16 to 19GB and the 31B version at 19 to 20GB. OpenAI's gpt-oss-20b needs only 16GB of memory, and gpt-oss-120b runs within 80GB, according to OpenAI.

LM Studio is probably the go-to choice because it provides a nice polished graphical user interface. Ollama, on the other hand, is aimed at developers and more experienced users who prefer working inside of a terminal (CLI).

For LM Studio, download the installer for your operating system from lmstudio.ai, search for a model inside the app, download its weights and open a chat. The app searches Hugging Face, the public hub where open models are published.

For Ollama, macOS and Linux users install with the command curl -fsSL https://ollama.com/install.sh | sh, and Windows users run irm https://ollama.com/install.ps1 | iex in PowerShell, both listed on Ollama's download page.

Typing ollama pull gemma4:e2b downloads a model and ollama run gemma4:e2b opens a chat, and /bye exits, per Ollama's quickstart. The program also opens a local API at http://localhost:11434, so other apps on your machine can use the model.

Google's Multi-Token Prediction drafters for Gemma 4, available on Ollama under Apache 2.0, speed up generation by up to 3x using speculative decoding, in which a small model proposes several tokens ahead and the large one checks them together.

Google said that "if the target model agrees with the draft, it accepts the entire sequence in a single forward pass," and the target model adds one extra token of its own in that same pass.

Local models gain web search and file access through MCP servers, short for Model Context Protocol, and LM Studio added support in version 0.3.17, according to the Decrypt guide.

The four-month average describes the most capable open models, and the largest are far past consumer hardware. Alibaba's Qwen3.8-Max has 2.4 trillion total parameters, with 95 billion active at any time, compared with the 80GB of memory that gpt-oss-120b runs within.

When a model spills out of VRAM into system RAM, responses may be a lot slower, and larger context windows need more memory. Gemma 4's small models offer a 128K-token context window, the amount of text a model can consider at once, and the larger ones 256K.

Cloud services need none of that planning, and the frontier models behind them still lead on the hardest tests.

If you want to use AI privately, our own review recommended "xPrivo with a self-hosted local model for day-to-day drafting that must not leave your machine," and Kagi "when you need web retrieval and accept the API call tradeoff."

#5159. North Korean hackers launder $242 million of Binance loot via Tron as FBI presses THORChain: analyst

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Relay and THORChain moved the largest shares of the loot off Tron, while Symbiosis and Chainflip said their screening had flaws. THORChain’s official front end has blocked the FBI-listed addresses.

North Korean hackers have laundered about $242 million of the funds stolen from Binance on the Tron blockchain in two days, onchain analyst and Security Alliance (SEAL) contributor SomaXBT said on X on Sunday, as the FBI presses THORChain and other services to block the transactions.

The attackers took about $628 million from Binance on Tron and had roughly $249 million left to move after Tether froze 378.7 million USDT, SomaXBT said in his thread. Most of the $242 million, about $194 million, left Tron through bridges and swap services. Another $26 million stayed on the network, while about $22 million went to fees, slippage and price moves, according to the analyst.

The funds moved through a variety of crypto bridges: Relay handled $48 million of the outflows, THORChain $44 million, Chainflip $32.1 million and Symbiosis $16 million, together about $140 million, SomaXBT said. Bitget Wallet handled another $41.7 million, but where that money ended up is still unresolved, the analyst said.

Binance lost about $6.3 billion on Thursday in the largest crypto hack on record. The FBI attributed the theft to the TraderTraitor group on Saturday in an alert issued with the U.S. Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation.

The FBI said the hackers were converting stolen assets "primarily into Ethereum" and expects the funds to be further laundered through DeFi protocols and instant swap services. It urged THORChain's THORNode operators, alongside RPC node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi services and other providers, to block transactions tied to the theft. The agency also published a list of 608 Ethereum addresses, 19 bitcoin addresses and one Solana address, but no Tron addresses, that it said were linked to the attack.

A 2022 joint advisory from the FBI, the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency and the Treasury Department describes TraderTraitor as a North Korean state-sponsored hacking group, active since at least 2020, that steals and launders crypto "to support the North Korean regime." The advisory says the cybersecurity industry tracks the group under various designations, including Lazarus Group, APT38 and BlueNoroff.

The FBI also blamed TraderTraitor for the $1.5 billion Bybit hack in February 2025. The agency's recent alert did not disclose the evidence that led it to its attribution, though blockchain analysis firms Chainalysis, Elliptic and TRM Labs had already linked the theft to North Korea through onchain ties to earlier North Korean hacks.

THORChain technical co-founder Chad Barraford wrote on X on Saturday evening that "THORNode operators have zero information that isn't already public and readily available in real time." He added, "No subpoenas required, a best case scenario for investigations," but his posts since the hack have not said whether node operators would block the listed addresses.

Earlier on Saturday, an Ethereum reorg caused THORChain to double-refund a 10 ETH deposit, which triggered an emergency pause, Chainalysis said. The pause, somewhat ironically, stopped all users, including the hackers, from swapping through most of THORChain's bridges, according to the firm.

THORChain trading remained halted on 10 of its 13 connected chains, including Tron, Ethereum and Bitcoin, as of 1:20 p.m. ET Sunday, according to THORChain node data. Trading from Tron has been halted since around midday Saturday.

In a message to The Block on Sunday, Barraford said he does not talk to node operators much and had not seen any discussion of the FBI’s request. "I assume they will be consistent and resume trading as normal," he said, adding that "the official thorchain centralized frontend has blocked the addresses requested by the FBI."

The attackers have sent 185.1 million TRX and 3.83 million USDT into THORChain in 2,697 deposits, SomaXBT said. THORChain has paid out 17,019 ETH to 170 addresses and 53.25 BTC to 20 addresses.

The attackers lost about 12% to fees and slippage, and front-end interfaces earned about 145,800 RUNE, roughly $95,000, in affiliate fees. North Korea's Lazarus Group also used THORChain to move much of the ether stolen from Bybit's $1.5 billion hack onto bitcoin in February 2025.

Relay received 37.64 million USDT and 39.28 million TRX in 735 deposits, SomaXBT said, adding that the protocol "barely featured" in laundering from past TraderTraitor hacks. Onchain analyst Specter said on Saturday that the hackers bridged $38 million through Relay in seven hours, despite Relay's statement that it screens transaction addresses using third-party risk data, including from Chainalysis, and an internal blocklist.

One Ethereum address received 2,804 ETH and 1.02 million USDT from Relay's router and solvers in 97 payments over about 80 minutes on Saturday, Etherscan data shows, matching SomaXBT's figures. The wallet swapped the USDT for ether on CoW Protocol and sent about 3,257 ETH, roughly $7.4 million, to four new addresses. None of the five addresses is on the FBI's list.

On Sunday, the hackers separately began swapping 970,522 SOL, worth about $95.9 million, for bitcoin through Relay, Lookonchain reported. The hackers' Solana wallet, the only Solana address on the FBI's list, moved 486,970 SOL ($46.4 million) in the hour before Lookonchain's 5:35 a.m. ET post. The Relay-powered swaps began after the FBI listed the wallet on Saturday. The wallet held about 466,700 SOL at publication time, the same balance as at 9:55 a.m. ET, per Solscan data.

Chainflip received 49.47 million USDT in 571 deposits, but many swaps failed, SomaXBT said. Failed swaps were refunded to fresh Tron wallets and sent back in, 17.91 million USDT in all. Chainflip planned an upgrade last year to block the Bybit hackers from its cross-chain DEX.

Chainflip said in a statement to The Block that it screens every deposit in real time against several anti-money laundering and blockchain analytics providers, whose lists include the addresses in the FBI alert, and rejects funds based on where they came from. Rejected deposits were refunded to fresh addresses that its providers did not flag as linked to the hack, so "when previously rejected funds were pushed back in from those addresses, some of them passed screening," said Shaun Janse van Vuuren, Chainflip’s head of growth and strategy.

Chainflip said it is working with its providers to tag refund addresses as linked to the original source immediately, a change it said has closed the workaround.

Symbiosis said early Sunday it had identified attempts to move Binance hack funds through its protocol and was in contact with Binance. "Our screening is built for normal flows, not for tracking fast-moving, sophisticated attempts so some mechanisms have gaps," Symbiosis said on X. "We're closing them as we go."

The attackers sent 49.71 million TRX into Symbiosis in 1,367 smaller deposits, SomaXBT said. Symbiosis recovered 15 BTC after an exploit of its own bitcoin bridge in September.

The hackers' main Tron distribution wallet paid out every hour for 31 straight hours and never paused for longer than 46 minutes, blockchain analytics firm Elliptic said on Saturday. The same swap amounts kept recurring in THORChain deposits, one of them 19 times.

When a bridge rejected deposits, new wallets automatically re-sent the funds to the same destination, Elliptic said. In the Bybit case, laundering followed human working patterns, according to the firm.

Elliptic co-founder Tom Robinson said in comments shared with The Block that the activity does not appear to involve AI agents. "The pattern is repetitive and static: the same sizes, the same rhythm, the same routes, hour after hour," Robinson said. He added that test transactions and round-number top-ups point to "operators making decisions, with scripts doing the repetitive work."

Elliptic has verified $2.85 billion of the stolen funds as frozen or seized, about 44% of the $6.43 billion it counts as stolen, and 94% of that is stablecoins. No funds have been frozen on Bitcoin, the XRP Ledger, Solana or NEAR. About $3.25 billion, including 18,327 BTC and 233 million XRP, is held in assets that no issuer can freeze, the firm said.

Tether's frozen USDT is split across two attacker wallets, with 312.7 million in one and 65.99 million in the other, per Tronscan data. Mempool.space data checked by The Block shows all 18,270 BTC still sitting in the 19 bitcoin addresses on the FBI's list.

Chainalysis said on Saturday the hackers have been depositing stablecoins into DeFi protocols on Tron for depository receipt tokens, which cannot be frozen, and later redeeming them from new addresses. It did not name the protocol, which other onchain analysts identified as JustLend, and did not address a question from The Block about whether it was JustLend.

TRM Labs said 300 million USDT moved to a Tron wallet that "began supplying it to the JustLend lending protocol." Tronscan data shows that wallet put 10,000 USDT into JustLend, swapped about 234 million USDT for TRX on decentralized exchanges and sent 65.99 million USDT to one of the two frozen wallets.

The Block has reached out to Relay and Bitget Wallet for comment.

Updated on Nov. 2 at 10:40 a.m. ET with statements from Chainflip and Chad Barraford.

#5157. Spot bitcoin ETFs see largest daily outflows in four months following record Binance hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

US spot bitcoin ETFs shed $643 million on Friday, marking their largest single-day outflow since June 25.

U.S. spot bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) exchange-traded funds recorded their largest daily net outflows in four months on Friday, following Thursday's $6.3 billion hack at Binance.

According to SoSoValue data, spot bitcoin ETFs saw $643.3 million in net outflows on Friday, marking their largest single-day outflow since June 25. Their outflows for the week ending Oct. 30 amounted to $202.6 million.

Spot Ethereum Ethereum (ETH) ETFs saw $155.9 million in net outflows on Friday, their largest since Oct. 7. The ether funds reported $431.5 million in weekly outflows.

The ETF outflows came after Binance was hit by the largest crypto hack to date. Attackers, whom the FBI has identified as North Korean hackers, drained about $6.3 billion from Binance's hot wallets on Thursday.

The crypto exchange said it recorded roughly $3.64 billion in net outflows in the first nine and a half hours after it began restoring withdrawals on Sunday.

Bitcoin rose 1% over the past 24 hours to trade at $78,491 as of 11:20 p.m. ET Sunday, while ether climbed 1.3% to $2,296, according to The Block's crypto price page.

Jeff Ko, chief analyst of ViaBTC, told The Block that he views the ETF outflows as more of a "sentiment shock."

"Spot ETF holders have no direct exposure to Binance, since the underlying BTC is held with regulated custodians. And in context, a roughly $640 million outflow is actually modest against the total ETF assets," said Ko.

Rick Maeda, head of markets at Laevitas, shared similar views, noting that Friday's outflow was relatively small compared with the ETFs' total net assets of $98.14 billion.

"In scale, the $643 million outflow is still under 1% of spot BTC ETF assets so this was simply a de-risking day, and not capitulation or a loss in institutional confidence," said Maeda.

Still, analysts expect investor caution to persist in the near term.

"Taking a look at a similar precedent of Bybit's Q1 2025 hack, I would expect stabilization or modest outflows in the near term rather than a snap-back as long as the hack can be contained at its current situation," Maeda added.

Dominick John, analyst of Zeus Research, also said that the near-term demand is likely to remain cautious, "with a sustained recovery in ETF inflows dependent on improved market stability, restored liquidity and stronger investor confidence."

The Crypto Fear and Greed Index stood at 37 on Sunday in the fear territory.

"The current sentiment in crypto seems to be one of caution rather than fear," said Maeda. "Markets will be headline-sensitive as traders look for updates regarding the hack as well as the macro calendar such as the U.S. midterms."

Ko also pointed to the midterm elections as another key event.

"BTC has absorbed the hack quite resiliently," said Ko. "Attention is now shifting toward U.S. midterm elections, where the balance of power in Congress could have meaningful implications for how crypto market structure legislation moves forward."

#5154. ‘We found room for even more orange’: Michael Saylor’s Strategy scoops up another 811 bitcoin for $65.5 million

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Strategy bought another 811 BTC for approximately $65.5 million last week for an average price of $80,846 per bitcoin.

Bitcoin Bitcoin (BTC) treasury company Strategy Strategy, Inc. (MSTR) picked up another 811 BTC for roughly $65.5 million at an average price of $80,846 per bitcoin between Oct. 26 and Nov. 1, according to an 8-K filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission on Monday.

As a result, Strategy's total holdings now stand at 849,327 BTC ($65.9 billion), acquired for a total cost of around $64.1 billion at an average price of $75,451 per bitcoin, and representing more than 4% of bitcoin's total supply cap.

The latest acquisitions used proceeds from at-the-market sales of Strategy's Class A common stock, MSTR, following a pause in acquisitions the week before. Strategy sold 1,178,552 MSTR shares for approximately $164 million during the period, with $18.62 billion worth of MSTR shares left available for issuance and sale under that ATM program, according to the filing.

Strategy also repurchased nearly 2.1 million STRC preferred shares last week for around $208.2 million, using its USD Cash reserve. The company also used $43.4 million from its USD Reserve to fund dividends on its preferred stock.

As of Nov. 1, Strategy's USD Reserve and USD Cash balances stood at $4.79 billion and $132.7 million, respectively, according to the firm.

On Thursday, Strategy posted $19.5 billion in net income for the third quarter, up from $2.79 billion during the same quarter last year, with the rising value of its bitcoin holdings generating a $20.9 billion gain.

"We're at the beginning of the next bitcoin bull market," Executive Chairman Michael Saylor said at the time. "Banks are embracing bitcoin, bitcoin's dominance among digital assets is growing, institutional demand is back, and bitcoin price is 21% above its 200-week moving average. Digital Credit continues to grow, with $16 billion in notional value across ten publicly listed securities."

Meanwhile, Strategy said CFO Andrew Kang will step down on Nov. 5, with current COO Reza Virasteh set to take on the role in January.

Earlier in the week, STRC, STRF, STRK and STRD preferred shareholders approved a change allowing dividends to accrue daily, rather than on the previous fortnightly schedule, with the amendment receiving 99.3% of votes cast.

Analysts at StoneX and TD Cowen said Strategy is in a stronger position to buy more bitcoin after stabilizing its preferred-stock financing and improving its liquidity.

Saylor posted another Strategy bitcoin acquisition tracker chart on Sunday, saying, "We found room for even more orange," indicating the latest acquisition announcement in advance of Monday's filing.

Strategy is the largest of 199 public companies that have some form of bitcoin treasury model. Metaplanet, Tether-backed Twenty One, Strive, and MARA represent the rest of the top five, with 44,000 BTC, 43,514 BTC, 43,000 BTC, and 35,577 BTC, respectively, according to Bitcoin Treasuries data.

Strategy's stock dropped 9.6% last week, closing at $130.51 on Friday, according to The Block's MSTR price page. Bitcoin fell 3.6% over the same period amid the record $6.3 billion Binance hack.

#5153. Bitcoin ETF Investors Head for the Exits After Record Binance Hack

Bitcoin ETF Investors Head for the Exits After Record Binance Hack

The first real test of how ETF investors would react to the largest crypto hack in history came back red.

U.S. spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.2 million on Friday, according to Decrypt’s ETF tracker, their biggest single-day outflow of October. It was the funds’ first full session since attackers began draining roughly $6.4 billion from Binance at 4:05 p.m. ET Thursday, after U.S. markets had closed. Thursday’s $17.2 million in inflows predated the theft.

The selling erased a four-day, $441 million inflow run, leaving the funds down about $202 million for the week and roughly $516 million for October. Cumulative net inflows since launch slipped to $57.4 billion, with total net assets at $103.2 billion.

Ethereum and Solana ETFs fared worse relative to their size. Ethereum funds lost $155.7 million on Friday, extending a five-day outflow streak to about $431 million and pushing October losses to roughly $1.61 billion. Solana ETFs shed $10.8 million, their fifth straight day of outflows, totaling about $72 million for the week. They logged net inflows on just two days all month.

Spot crypto ETFs hold the underlying asset, such as Bitcoin or Ethereum, and trade on stock exchanges like ordinary shares, giving investors exposure through a regular brokerage account.

Bitcoin funds have drawn $57.4 billion in net inflows since debuting in January 2024, and Ethereum funds have taken in $12.2 billion since launching that July. Because issuers report flows daily and much of the money comes from institutions and financial advisers, the figures have become a closely watched gauge of investor sentiment.

Crypto markets initially appeared to take the Binance hack largely in stride, considering the scale of the theft. Bitcoin fell about 4% to roughly $76,400 on Friday, and more than $1.3 billion in leveraged positions were liquidated. It’s a sharp drop, but a far smaller one than the nearly 11% plunge after a failed attack on Binance in 2018.

Binance has pledged to cover all losses with company funds and an emergency reserve, while Tether and Circle froze more than $2.3 billion in stolen stablecoins. Some Wall Street firms even urged clients to buy crypto-linked stocks on the potential weakness in markets.

By Saturday, Bitcoin had steadied at about $77,520, up 0.5% over 24 hours but down 3.3% on the week. Ethereum traded near $2,251, down 3.9% for the week, and Solana near $96, down 4.5%. Total crypto market cap held at about $2.7 trillion.

Macro headwinds remain in play. The Federal Reserve held rates at 3.75% to 4% on Wednesday, but two officials voted for a hike, and traders had priced odds above 70% for an increase in December.

Traders on Myriad, a prediction market developed by Decrypt’s parent company Dastan, have turned bearish on Ethereum and Solana. They now give Ethereum a 51.3% chance of falling to $1,500 before reaching $3,000, flipping a market that favored the upside at 61.3% a week ago. Solana’s odds of sinking to $40 before hitting $160 rose to 53.7%.

#5151. Tether Holds 150,000 Bitcoin CEO Says, 50,000 More Than Its Reserve Report Shows

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said in Lugano that the company holds about 150,000 bitcoin worth $11.5 billion.

Tether holds about 150,000 bitcoin, worth some $11.5 billion at current prices, CEO Paolo Ardoino said, a figure that exceeds the holdings implied by the company’s latest reserve attestation by roughly 50,000 coins.

“We have give or take 150,000 bitcoin ourselves. It’s not much, but it’s honest work,” Ardoino said during his keynote at the Plan ₿ Forum in Lugano, Switzerland, the city where Tether has run a bitcoin and stablecoin adoption program since 2022.

At that size the stack would rank Tether among the largest corporate holders of bitcoin, behind Strategy’s 848,517 BTC (as of Q3) and ahead of most public miners and treasury companies.

Ardoino did not say where the additional coins sit, when they were bought or whether they belong to the stablecoin issuer or to Tether Investments, the arm that holds the group’s venture and equity positions. The remark does not indicate a new 50,000 BTC purchase.

JUST IN: Tether now holds approximately 150,000 Bitcoin worth $11.5 billion, CEO Paolo Ardoino announces 👀 pic.twitter.com/s7HLmXwkQE

Tether International’s third-quarter reserve report, published Oct. 30 with an attestation from BDO, lists $8.37 billion in bitcoin valued at $83,621.23 per coin as of Sept. 30. That implies about 100,060 BTC, up around 1,130 from the roughly 98,930 held at the end of June. Most of the quarter’s $2.57 billion rise in the bitcoin line came from price, not purchases.

The gap between the report and Ardoino’s figure has a plausible home. Tether has said the investments it makes from profits are kept apart from the reserves backing USDT, so coins held by Tether Investments would not appear in the attestation.

Tether’s bitcoin exposure extends past its own wallets. It took full control of Twenty One Capital in May after buying out SoftBank, backs Adecoagro’s mining in Brazil, and runs mining and energy ventures in Latin America. Whether any of those positions sit inside the 150,000 figure is unclear.

The disclosure lands as Tether’s balance sheet grows more dependent on the two assets. The Q3 report shows $191.45 billion in reserve assets against $183.50 billion in liabilities, an excess of $7.95 billion, up from $4.11 billion in June when the cushion had halved.

Bitcoin at $8.37 billion and gold at about $19.1 billion together exceed that buffer several times over, so a sharp drop in either would eat into it. Treasury bills remain the bulk of reserves at about $132.4 billion.

Tether earned about $1.5 billion in the quarter and $4.5 billion in the first nine months. In August it completed its first independent audit with KPMG, ending years of reliance on attestations alone. Ardoino said at the forum that USDT has more than 730 million users and is adding about 30 million wallets a quarter.

Bitcoin traded near $77,600 Tuesday, which puts Tether’s stated holdings at $11.6 billion and its attested holdings at about $7.8 billion, below the Sept. 30 mark. The company has not said whether it plans to publish a breakdown of the coins held outside the reserve.

#5148. Bitmine adds 8,573 ETH as Tom Lee flags muted Binance hack reaction

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The company reported $14.5 billion in total crypto, cash, and, marketable securities as of Nov. 1.

Bitmine Immersion Technologies increased its Ethereum treasury to 6,060,506 ETH after acquiring 8,573 ETH over the past week, the company said in a statement on Monday.

At $2,261 per ETH, the holdings were worth approximately $13.7 billion as of Nov. The haul represents 4.9% of ether's total supply, according to the statement.

Of the total, Bitmine has staked 5,227,037 ETH worth $11.8 billion, representing approximately 86% of its position. The company projects $358 million in annualized staking revenue at current levels, rising to $415 million if its ETH were fully staked.

Beyond ether, Bitmine said it holds 216 BTC, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries, an $82 million investment in Eightco Holdings, and $498 million in cash and marketable securities. Its combined crypto, cash, marketable securities, and other investments totaled $14.5 billion.

Chairman Tom Lee also addressed the recent $6.3 billion Binance hack. He said ETH had declined approximately 4% since the incident was disclosed, compared the decline with an 8% drop on the day the $1.5 billion Bybit theft was announced in February 2025. ETH subsequently fell 20% within a week of the Bybit incident.

"While this is a terrible development, in my view, the relatively muted crypto market reaction is very constructive, particularly compared to the market's response to prior sizable crypto thefts," Lee said.

Lee cited six factors for the muted price reaction, including Binance's ability to cover losses, the absence of decentralized finance protocol failures, and swift industry intervention that limited losses by 50%.

ETH traded at $2,281.41 on Monday, up 0.87% over 24 hours, according to The Block's price page.

Bitmine shares closed Friday at $20.18, down 6.83%.

#5145. 30 Years of Legal Weed: Has It Made Us Any Healthier?

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

The very highs and bizarre lows of America on medicinal marijuana.

EARLIER THIS YEAR, during a brief and mercifully abandoned period when I was considering doing stand-up as a hobby, I went to a comedy show in downtown Austin. I was legitimately shocked to find, as part of the two-drink minimum bill of fare, the option for several different flavors of $5 THC seltzers. This wasn’t “take a pull off your vape or smoke a roach in the alley before the show” territory. No, marijuana drinks were on the menu.

In the past few years, my only social activities have been poker, competitive trivia, and going to the movies, all activities people do stoned, but not activities where you can literally order drugs from the bar alongside your chicken wings. The comedy club, on the other hand, considered marijuana products as a profit center.

This was one of those lightly attended weeknight shows where the comedians banter with the audience because they haven’t prepared much material. So, during a Q&A, I brought up the fact that there were THC sodas available to order, in laminate. “Sir, this is 2026,” the host said.

This is where we are now, a full three decades after California legalized medical weed. But is that good for us?

As I write this, it’s been eight-and-a-half years since I quit marijuana, a drug that at one point made pretty much anything—listening to music, shopping for groceries, cleaning the house—fun for me.

But really, I’ve been sober since I stopped using THC, since I was hardly just smoking there at the end. I was vaping, eating, drinking, dabbing, and otherwise gorging myself on what has become an extremely potent, psychosis-inducing drug. And I wasn’t having fun.

My breaking point, which I recounted in my 2020 memoir Pothead, came at Game 2 of the 2017 World Series at Dodger Stadium, where I was so wasted on weed that I had a screaming meltdown in front of security when I found out the ticket I’d bought was fraudulent.

It was a low point, a bottom so deep and so pathetic that I should have quit right there. But I went on for another three weeks before I finally crawled to a Marijuana Anonymous meeting and asked for help.

Now, nearly a decade later, I’ve emerged from my cocoon to find that THC is everywhere. I live in a state where it’s still technically illegal, but you can find some sort of THC variant in most convenience stores and in most bars and nightclubs.

My friend is an attorney who represents the Texas Alcoholic Beverage Commission. He says his client is in a near panic. Sales are down for every beverage except for nonalcoholic mixers and those THC seltzers. THC is the beer, wine, and vodka of the present day, combined.

And so we find ourselves in a bizarre situation where people are drinking way less alcohol because it’s unhealthy for you, but they are instead ingesting THC products—either through soda, gummies, or any number of other concoctions—from unknown manufacturers, with unknown formulas and malleable dosages.

They’re also vaping, which emerging research is showing is kind of awful for our lungs. A University of Michigan study recently showed that teens who vaped cannabis were twice as likely to experience respiratory symptoms like “wheezing or whistling” in the chest than those who vaped nicotine or smoked traditional cigarettes.

Unlike traditional marijuana, this stuff isn’t “from the earth.” Who knows where it’s really from—or where it’s going?

When I quit weed in 2017, and somewhat loudly and publicly announced myself as an addict, I got the requisite amount of sympathy, but also a tremendous amount of pushback. Marijuana isn’t dangerous, people said. It isn’t addictive. It’s actually good for you.

In 2016, Gavin Newsom, then California’s lieutenant governor, spearheaded the legalization push for recreational marijuana. Cannabis sales have produced more than $8 billion in tax revenue for California since retail sales began in 2018, with a decent proportion of that funneled into teen substance-abuse prevention programs. But the state has also seen an increase in children suffering from cannabis poisoning.

It’s been a massive shift, moving from medically administered weed for veterans and cancer patients to powerful THC beverages you can buy at your bodega.

Legalization has created a new frontier of intoxicants without a centralized warning system for users. Data from a 2025 national study of U.S. adults stated that 15 percent of respondents reported daily or near-daily cannabis use.

Among those daily users, 18 percent reported experiencing symptoms consistent with cannabinoid hyperemesis syndrome, a disorder characterized by cyclic, severe nausea, intractable vomiting, and gut pain alleviated temporarily by hot baths or showers.

This translates to an estimated 7.2 million people in the United States alone, many of them teenagers, experiencing these symptoms. That number is probably vastly underestimated, given that fewer than 12 percent of sufferers have received a formal diagnosis.

Welcome to the darker side of legalized weed, a world of cannabis-induced psychosis, “scromiting” (yes, screaming while vomiting), and concoctions like “shatter” or “wax,” which contain up to 90 percent THC, as opposed to the 3 to 5 percent in joints from previous decades.

So I find myself in a somewhat odd situation. I was a relatively early victim of modern weed’s potency, as well as a relatively early unadopter, and a very early voice in the wilderness warning the world that marijuana might not be the answer to all our health concerns. But now I find myself, like a patient waking up from a long coma, in a world where THC is as prevalent as, and cheaper than, whiskey. Yet still, I find myself thinking it should be legal. So which is it? Maybe we still don’t have enough data.

Thirty years since the start of legalization might feel like a long time, but it’s really not. In terms of scientific analysis, we’re still in relatively undiscovered country. If you’re suffering from chronic pain, or dying of cancer, marijuana is a healthier option than, say, oxycodone. But for the rest of us? No one really knows.

The only thing I can be truly sure of is that it’s no longer for me.

#5144. Strive inches closer to becoming second-largest public bitcoin holder after latest $10 million buy

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Strive has acquired another 124 BTC for approximately $10 million, bringing its total holdings to 43,124 BTC.

Bitcoin treasury company Strive Strive, Inc. (ASST) bought another 124 Bitcoin (BTC) between Oct. 24 and Oct. 30, bringing its total treasury to 43,124 BTC, according to a Monday filing with the Securities and Exchange Commission.

The acquisition represents a slowdown compared to Strive's prior buys in recent weeks, picking up around $1.25 billion worth of the largest cryptocurrency in October alone. Nevertheless, it is inching closer to CEO Matt Cole's long-stated aim of becoming the second-largest public bitcoin holder by year-end. At current holdings, it would need to acquire fewer than 100 BTC per week for the rest of 2026 to overtake Metaplanet, assuming the Japanese company makes no further purchases.

Strategy continues to dominate the rankings with 849,327 BTC, while Metaplanet, Twenty One, and MARA make up the rest of the top five, with 44,000 BTC, 43,514 BTC, and 35,577 BTC, respectively, per Bitcoin Treasuries data. Strive currently sits in fourth place.

Strive ended Oct. 30 with $490.4 million in cash and cash equivalents, down $14 million from a week earlier, according to its latest SEC filing. In a separate post on X, Cole said the company funded most of its latest bitcoin purchases with existing cash and also used its SATS preferred stock at-the-market offering program for the first time.

Strive launched the program on Oct. 26, allowing it to sell up to $1 billion of SATS preferred shares to fund additional bitcoin purchases and other corporate expenses.

Strive also continues to hold 505,000 shares of Strategy's STRC preferred stock, valued at approximately $50.1 million as of Oct. 30. Its SATA preferred shares outstanding remained unchanged at just over 14 million.

#5143. FBI Blames North Korea for $6B Binance Hack as Exchange Reopens Withdrawals

FBI Blames North Korea for $6B Binance Hack as Exchange Reopens Withdrawals

The FBI has confirmed that North Korea was behind last week's theft of more than $6 billion from Binance, as the exchange began reopening withdrawals and reported billions of dollars leaving the platform.

In an alert published on Saturday, the FBI said the attack was carried out by North Korean hackers it refers to as "TraderTraitor," confirming the attribution blockchain analytics firms TRM Labs and Chainalysis made on Friday.

The FBI is releasing information advising the Democratic People's Republic of Korea (North Korea) was responsible for the theft of more than $6 billion USD in virtual assets from cryptocurrency exchange, Binance, on or about October 29, 2026. The FBI refers to this specific North… pic.twitter.com/TBUvUFln4n

— FBI Cyber Branch (@FBICyberBranch) October 31, 2026

The FBI, together with the Secret Service and IRS Criminal Investigation, has seen the hackers converting stolen assets mainly into Ethereum, and expects them to launder the funds further through DeFi and instant swap services, the bureau said. It urged node operators, instant exchanges, DeFi services, blockchain analytics firms and other crypto businesses to block transactions involving hundreds of Ethereum addresses, 19 Bitcoin addresses and one Solana address it linked to the theft.

The Secret Service, which is also investigating the theft, said on Sunday that its investigators worked with industry partners to identify and freeze "a significant portion" of the stolen crypto within minutes of the attack. That work continues with the FBI, IRS Criminal Investigation, the U.S. Attorney's Offices for Washington, D.C. and the Central District of California, and the Justice Department's computer crime and national security divisions, the agency said.

The laundering is already well underway. After the attack, the hackers moved more than $500 million on Tron through more than 1,000 addresses, over 3,000 deposits and several Tron-based DeFi protocols, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said on Sunday. Most of those staging wallets have now been drained, with funds leaving Tron through cross-chain services including Chainflip, Relay, THORChain and NEAR for dozens of fresh wallets on Ethereum, Bitcoin and other chains, and the launderers have started moving onto Solana, the firm said.

Binance began restoring withdrawals in phases at 1 a.m. ET on Sunday, and all 15 networks and assets in its first phase were available by 7:36 a.m. ET. In the first nine and a half hours, it processed $4.649 billion in withdrawals and $1.01 billion in deposits, a net outflow of about $3.64 billion, the exchange said.

That represents 2.1% of the $171 billion in assets Binance reported in its most recent proof of reserves, from October 28, according to the exchange. "After an initial spike, withdrawal volumes have declined and are stabilising as expected," Binance said. It took a new reserves snapshot on October 31 and expects to publish an updated report on Monday.

As withdrawals began, CEO Richard Teng said Binance had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets. "Binance will absorb the financial impact of the stolen funds, not our users," he wrote.

Today, we're beginning the phased resumption of withdrawals, taking a careful approach to ensure services return safely and securely.

We've now replenished approximately 95% of the impacted assets, and our commitment remains clear: Binance will absorb the financial impact of the… https://t.co/wKvI7Q4fsT pic.twitter.com/uWFGs3sJJ5

— Richard Teng (@_RichardTeng) November 1, 2026

Binance has since reopened withdrawals on more networks, including Zcash, TON and Sui, and on Monday restored fiat withdrawals in U.S. dollars, UAE dirhams, Vietnamese dong, Philippine pesos and Indonesian rupiah.

"Our top priority right now is to ensure the environment is secure, after which users can withdraw their funds and Binance can resume operations," Binance co-CEO Yi He said on X on Monday, Reuters reported. Binance said it has raised $6 billion to cover the losses from its corporate treasury, an insurance fund and its SAFU emergency fund, according to Reuters, which also reported that Circle had frozen more than $1.1 billion in USDC.

Data from DefiLlama, which tracks the public wallets of exchanges, put Binance's total assets at about $127.6 billion on Monday. More than $6 billion left wallets associated with Binance over 24 hours after withdrawals reopened, CoinDesk reported, citing DefiLlama, though those movements can include transfers between Binance's own wallets. Rival exchanges OKX and Bybit recorded net inflows of about $450 million and $134 million, respectively, during Binance's reopening.

The hack has also rattled institutional investors. Spot Bitcoin ETFs shed $643.2 million on Friday, the first full trading session after the hack, leaving the funds with roughly $516 million in net outflows for October. "A hack at a crypto exchange is bank robbery at the speed of the internet," TRM Labs' Ari Redbord told Reuters.

#5140. Binance completes latest proof of reserves as it works to restore assets, withdrawals after record hack

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance is planning another reserves snapshot on Wednesday and said it expects to begin progressively restoring withdrawals for additional networks and assets on Tuesday.

Binance said it has completed a new zero-knowledge proof of reserves snapshot, allowing users holding BTC, ETH, USDT, BNB, SOL, USDC, XRP, USD1, RLUSD and ZEC to independently verify that their balances are backed 1:1.

In its latest recovery update published Monday morning, the exchange said it replenished about 95% of the assets affected by last week's hack with its own funds, with the remaining 5% in less-liquid tokens it is still working to acquire.

"We've completed the latest Proof of Reserves verification, giving users another way to independently check that their assets are backed," Binance Co-CEO Richard Teng wrote in a post on X. "There's still work to do, and we'll continue to communicate our progress openly."

Binance plans another reserves snapshot on Wednesday and said it expects to begin progressively restoring withdrawals for additional networks and assets on Tuesday.

"Computation is required from the snapshot to the updated full zero-knowledge proof, and the generation time depends on the number of tokens," Binance co-founder and co-CEO Yi He said on X. "It is currently estimated that generating the zero-knowledge proof will take about 40 to 50 hours after the snapshot is completed."

In the largest known crypto exploit on record, onchain investigators say that North Korean attackers exploited Binance's internal systems and wallet-signing mechanisms to move more than $6 billion in funds across multiple blockchains. On Sunday, SomaXBT said North Korean hackers laundered about $242 million of the funds stolen from Binance on Tron.

"The response from issuers, decentralized finance (DeFi) protocols and Binance itself was faster and better coordinated than anything the industry had previously managed, which we regard as a point of validation for several of the business models we cover, regulated custody most of all," StoneX analysts wrote Monday in a note to clients.

Binance's 24-hour net outflows fell to approximately $3.29 billion, down more than 50% from an earlier peak of around $7 billion, according to DeFiLlama. The data tracker estimates that Binance holds approximately $127.78 billion in assets across its linked onchain wallets.
