#5138. DTCC’s tokenization launch could accelerate adoption and boost Securitize, StoneX says

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

DTCC's permissioned service targets different markets than Securitize's public-blockchain tokenization, StoneX analysts said, keeping a buy rating on SECZ.

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation's (DTCC) new tokenization service validates rather than threatens Securitize's business model, according to StoneX analysts, who argue that the two platforms serve different markets.

While DTCC's permissioned service is initially limited to certain securities, participants and blockchain networks, Securitize (SECZ) focuses on public-blockchain tokenization, including tokenized funds, equities and assets that can be used in decentralized finance.

"We acknowledge that DTCC has opened a debate about where tokenized U.S. equities should ultimately be anchored," analyst Mark Palmer wrote Monday in a note to clients. "At the same time, we believe SECZ is well positioned to emerge as a winner of that debate because it is the only tokenization platform that already holds the full regulatory stack that an issuer needs to sponsor its own on-chain shares, an SEC-registered transfer agent, broker-dealer and ATS."

StoneX also pointed to the potential for tokenized assets to serve as collateral across markets, arguing that the ability to move tokenized Treasuries around the clock could drive demand for tokenized fund shares and equities as collateral. Securitize is well positioned to benefit, the analysts said, given its regulatory infrastructure and role as a transfer agent for a large pool of tokenized assets.

That use case is already developing in decentralized finance. Tokenized private credit on Solana reached $1.44 billion, with credit tokens from Securitize and Maple being accepted as collateral in lending markets such as Kamino, according to The Block's latest Tokenization Briefing.

Securitize is also expanding its tokenized equity business. On Monday, the company expanded its tokenized U.S. stock offering to 25 equities, adding 13 companies, including ExxonMobil, GameStop, Salesforce and Disney, to its existing lineup. The tokens trade on Solana and are backed 1:1 by underlying shares, providing eligible investors with associated economic benefits and rights, including dividends and voting rights where applicable.

Last month, StoneX argued that Securitize Stocks could help the company move beyond integration fees and generate transaction and servicing revenue, with collateral-related revenue a potential longer-term opportunity.

The firm reiterated its "buy" rating and $18 price target on SECZ shares. The stock traded around $7.30 at publication time, down 24% since going public in July 2025.

#5137. BTCPay Tells Merchants to Switch Off Servers Amid Hot Wallet Attack Reports

BTCPay Tells Merchants to Switch Off Servers Amid Hot Wallet Attack Reports

BTCPay Server, a free, open-source tool businesses use to accept Bitcoin payments, told operators to switch off their servers on Sunday after reports that attackers were draining the hot wallets connected to them.

"We are receiving reports of hot wallets on BTCPay Servers being swept by unauthorized attackers," the project tweeted at 5:25 p.m. ET. "If you're running an instance, please shut it down (./btcpay-down.sh) until we complete the investigation."

🚨 We are receiving reports of hot wallets on BTCPay Servers being swept by unauthorized attackers.

If you're running an instance, please shut it down (./btcpay-down.sh) until we complete the investigation.

— BTCPay Server (@BtcpayServer) November 1, 2026

About two hours later, BTCPay founder Nicolas Dorier said the reports appeared to involve releases older than version 2.4.2, and that the attacker had logged in to affected servers with administrator access.

Later on Sunday, the project said its investigation had identified an attack vector targeting instances running version 2.4.1, released on July 23, or older. "We have no credible reports of hot wallet drains for users running the latest 2.4.7 release," it said, urging everyone to keep their software updated and asking affected users to send details and logs to its security team.

Some hosting providers, including LunaNode, have shut down BTCPay instances automatically to stop older versions from being exploited, the project said. BTCPay has not said how many servers were affected or how much Bitcoin was taken.

Because BTCPay is self-hosted, each merchant runs the software on their own server, and there is no central operator that can switch instances off. Merchants can choose to run a hot wallet, which stores the wallet's seed on that internet-connected server so it can sign transactions automatically. BTCPay's own documentation labels the feature "DANGER" and warns that it "comes with security implications."

The attacks come days after BTCPay released version 2.4.7 on Thursday as an emergency update fixing one critical and several medium-severity vulnerabilities, which it said were brought to its attention through Anthropic's frontier open-source software scanner. BTCPay has not said whether the attacks exploited any of those flaws.

It is not the first security scare for the project this year. In August, BTCPay warned that a critical vulnerability in all versions before 2.4.2 was being actively exploited, and told users to update or shut down their servers. Supporters of the project later funded a recovery bounty of 10% of any stolen funds returned, capped at 3 BTC.

#5135. This Is Your Brain on Weed. It’s Actually Kind of Amazing.

In November 1996, California became the first state to legalize medical cannabis. (Many) politics and (some) physicians promised us breakthroughs in better health. Thirty years later, how are we really doing? Men’s Health decided to find out in this special report package. Read more here.

WHEN YOU USE weed, your brain kind of goes all over the place. You likely know about how marijuana can chill you out, make you forget yourself, and induce the munchies. But there are other fascinating ways that the drug can alter your mind.

When THC binds to receptors in your hippocampus, the compound disrupts signals and impairs memory. That’s why you may forget what you were saying mid-sentence or where you put your keys when stoned.

THC also binds to receptors in the amygdala, which can help you feel chill when high—and paranoid or anxious when you’re too high.

The back of the brain, the cerebellum, is important for balance, motor skills, and time perception. When THC gets involved, you might feel clumsier and as if time is slowing.

THC disrupts the prefrontal cortex, which oversees critical thinking, problem-solving, and processing information from your senses. As a result, you’re going to find it harder to multitask, feel more impulsive, and zone out.

THC also stimulates the release of feel-good dopamine in the nucleus accumbens. If you’re a chronic, heavy user, cannabis can dull dopamine response and potentially rewire circuitry within the nucleus accumbens, making you more susceptible to addiction.

Your prefrontal cortex and limbic system contribute to imaginative ideas. THC can bind to receptors in these regions and may reduce self-critical noise, allowing for greater free expression.

Scientists believe that the anterior cingulate cortex is central to the perception of pain, and THC seems to dampen its ability—in a good way.

There’s so much more going on in the world of weed and health. Read more here.

Jocelyn Solis-Moreira, MS is the associate health & fitness for Men's Health and has previously written for CNN, Scientific American, Popular Science, and National Geographic before joining the brand. When she's not working, she's doing circus arts or working towards the perfect pull-up.

#5132. Mercado Bitcoin, Brazil’s largest crypto exchange, hit by $46 million security breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The exchange paused deposits and withdrawals after the incident, while Tether froze more than 4.5 million USDT linked to the attacker.

Mercado Bitcoin, Brazil's largest cryptocurrency exchange, confirmed Monday that it suffered a security incident involving approximately $46.3 million in assets, according to Arkham data.

"We identified and swiftly contained a security incident in one of our systems early this morning," the exchange said in a post on X. Mercado Bitcoin temporarily suspended deposits and withdrawals as a precaution while its teams investigate the incident, track the assets, and coordinate with authorities.

The exchange said the incident did not affect customers or result in a data breach. It also published the destination wallet addresses associated with the transactions to help blockchain security firms and other parties monitor and track the assets.

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino confirmed that the stablecoin issuer had frozen more than 4.5 million USDT linked to the incident.

Meanwhile, Arkham analyst Emmett Gallic said the attacker was sweeping funds from older deposit addresses and selling memecoins and other altcoins.

Mercado Bitcoin's incident comes amid a string of security breaches and exploits across the crypto industry in recent days, following Thursday's $6 billion hack of Binance. That same day, Polymarket was hit by a $2.65 million exploit, in which it said a third-party provider was compromised.

Late Friday evening, blockchain analytics firm Chainalysis said its own customer data was accessed by an AI-assisted hacker. Of note, the firm said the incident was not related to the theft at Binance.

On Saturday, hackers hijacked a dozen DeFi websites registered with Name.com and stole about $138,000. Blockaid said the sites were rigged with a malware kit that tricks visitors into signing away their funds.

#5128. Strategy Resumes Bitcoin Buys With $66M Purchase

Strategy Resumes Bitcoin Buys With $66M Purchase

Strategy bought 811 BTC for $65.5 million last week, the company said on Monday, resuming its Bitcoin purchases after a one-week pause.

The Bitcoin treasury firm paid an average of $80,846 per coin between October 26 and November 1, funding the purchases by selling 1,178,552 shares of MSTR stock for $164 million in net proceeds, according to an SEC filing. Of that, $65.5 million went to Bitcoin and $98.5 million to its USD Cash account.

Strategy has acquired 811 $BTC and repurchased $208M of $STRC. As of 11/1/26, we hold 849,327 BTC and $4.9B of USD Assets. $MSTRhttps://t.co/fk7kWJxf3L

Strategy now holds 849,327 BTC, acquired for a total of $64.08 billion at an average of about $75,451 per coin.

The company also bought back 2,092,035 shares of STRC, its variable-rate preferred stock, for $208.2 million, all paid from USD Cash. That is up from $204.9 million the previous week, when Strategy paused its Bitcoin buying for the first time since mid-September. The week before that, it bought 267 BTC for $22.1 million.

USD Cash, the pool Strategy keeps for general purposes including buying Bitcoin, fell to $132.7 million from $242.4 million a week earlier. Its separate USD Reserve, which covers preferred dividends and interest on its debt, stood at $4.79 billion after paying out $43.4 million in dividends. About $748.6 million remains available under its program for repurchasing preferred stock.

Strategy put its combined USD assets at $4.9 billion and its "USD Duration," its measure of how long those assets could cover dividends and interest, at 3.3 years, it tweeted.

The purchases come as STRC moves to a new dividend schedule. Shareholders approved daily dividends on Strategy's four preferred stocks last week, with STRC's first daily record date falling on November 1.

Mizuho Financial Group maintained its Outperform rating and $165 price target on MSTR following Strategy's third-quarter earnings call, pointing to a stronger balance sheet with net leverage down to 2.4% from 8.4%, according to a summary of the note. But analyst Dan Dolev also flagged dilution concerns, noting that Bitcoin per share has fallen 3.7% this year.

Strategy also faces possible removal from MSCI's equity indexes under a rule the index provider finalized in October, which treats companies dominated by a single asset as ineligible. MSCI will publish the list of affected companies on November 11.

#5127. Kim Jong-Un’s sister rejects FBI claims linking North Korea to record $6 billion Binance hack: reports

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

On Monday, Yonhap News Agency reported that hackers from North Korea were not behind the over $6 billion hack of Binance, citing the sister of North Korea's leader Kim Jong-Un.

A North Korean official has denied the country was behind the largest crypto hack on record, despite onchain investigators and the FBI attributing the $6 billion exploit to North Korean hackers.

On Monday, Yonhap News Agency reported that Kim Yo-jong, the sister of North Korean leader Kim Jong-Un and a senior government official, rejected the FBI's assessment that North Korea was responsible for last week's Binance hack.

"The FBI is trying to brand the DPRK as a 'cyber criminal state', claiming that it obtained information that a hacking organization 'supported' by the DPRK was involved in the incident," Kim said in a statement, according to Yonhap.

The FBI did not immediately respond to a request for comment from The Block.

Ahead of the attack last week, an attacker deployed and tested malicious code on three wallets before later executing the attack on Binance on Thursday. On Ethereum, the attacker used EIP-7702 to turn a Binance hot wallet into a sweeper controlled by the attacker. It prepared 50 pre-signed authorizations to ensure one would remain valid when executed, according to TRM Labs.

Three firms, including TRM Labs, attributed the attack to North Korean bad actors. Then, over the weekend, the FBI confirmed that North Korea was responsible for the hack. In its release, the FBI referred to this specific North Korean malicious cyber activity as "TraderTraitor."

Earlier on Monday, Binance published its first set of proof of reserves and said it replenished about 95% of the assets affected by the hack with its own funds.

#5124. Morning Minute: Binance Hack Leads to Mass Outflows While FBI Opens Probe

Morning Minute: Binance Hack Leads to Mass Outflows While FBI Opens Probe

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

💸 The Binance Hack Just Cost Bitcoin ETFs Their Whole Month

Through Thursday, October was a quiet, slightly positive month for Bitcoin ETFs. Then North Korea took $6.4 billion out of Binance five minutes after the closing bell, and Friday’s single session erased the entire month.

To recap the Binance hack, attackers drained roughly $6.4 billion from Binance’s hot wallets across about 10 blockchains, with most of it inside 20 minutes on Thursday afternoon. That’s more than four times the old record, the $1.5 billion Bybit theft in February 2025. Both firms attribute it to TraderTraitor, the North Korea-linked group that also hit Bybit.

The entry point has changed since Friday. Binance first blamed a compromised laptop belonging to a DevOps engineer, then found “additional points of breach.” On Ethereum, the attacker used 50 pre-signed authorizations to turn a Binance hot wallet into a contract that emptied itself—a failure of Binance’s own signing approvals, not of Ethereum. No private keys leaked. The attacker wiped its tooling on the way out.

Friday was the aftermath, and it was bloody. Bitcoin ETFs bled $643.3 million on Friday, the biggest day of the month, ending a four-session buying streak. That one day took October from +$127 million to −$515.9 million. September was +$2.65 billion. The ETH ETFs were worse: −$1.61 billion for October, their worst month since launch, and an 11-session losing streak into the weekend.

Now the biggest players in the U.S. government are reacting. CFTC Chairman Michael Selig told CNBC the hack “proves our exact point.” “The real issue here is that these are unregulated offshore exchanges, and they don’t have the same sorts of safeguards.” He’s pitching a “federal option”—let offshore venues register with the CFTC and meet US cybersecurity standards. He also notes the hole came in “from someone’s laptop,” not from a blockchain. The FBI and Secret Service have also both opened probes.

Binance CEO Richard Teng says the company covers every dollar from corporate funds and SAFU, with $6.34 billion already set aside. CZ: “It hurts, but we survive.” Withdrawals were turned back on over the weekend and over $3.6B has exited so far according to QCP.

So what to watch from here? Monday’s inflows (or outflows) will be telling. If Friday’s bleeding continues to Monday, it’s a signal that the pain might just be beginning. Also, watch Binance. They have to buy roughly $1.3 billion of BTC, ETH, and BNB on the open market and OTC to top off the reimbursement pool—a forced bid underneath a market that just got hit. And then there’s Selig and the FBI; be on the lookout from updates from those agencies as the Binance hack aftermath carries forward.

#5119. Consensys launches cross-ledger settlement and routing stack for institutional networks

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The Sovereign Network Interoperability stack supports securities settlement, cross-currency payments, and collateral mobility while allowing institutions to retain control of their ledgers.

Consensys on Monday announced a new technology stack designed to let banks and financial institutions settle transactions across independently operated blockchains without moving assets off their native ledgers.

Dubbed "Sovereign Network Interoperability," the product was launched at Devcon in Mumbai. It supports collateral mobility, securities settlement, wholesale and cross-currency payments, as well as  funds and asset servicing, according to a statement.

Consensys said the software stack includes a Cross Ledger Interoperability Protocol, built on a decade of research into zero-knowledge proofs. This architecture ensures bridgeless interoperability, meaning assets stay on their native ledgers while cross-ledger state and execution are verified cryptographically, it added.

Under the system, an institution specifies its transaction requirements and approved counterparties. The software selects an eligible route, coordinates execution on the participating ledgers and tracks the outcome.

"We are bringing deep protocol expertise, transaction coordination and complete support into one product," Consensys President David Cunningham said in the statement. "The result is a simpler path to each new connection, with verifiable outcomes and clear accountability for keeping the full stack working for years to come."

The product will also support the Ethereum Economic Zone standard, the Inter-Blockchain Communication protocol, and other interoperability technologies, per the statement. EVM-based networks are available now, while Canton connectivity is planned.

The launch follows Consensys' Oct. 8 partnership with ClearToken to develop infrastructure for round-the-clock settlement of tokenized securities and cash. In September, the company also announced plans to separate its consumer and institutional businesses, with MetaMask becoming an independent consumer-focused company and Consensys retaining its protocols and institutional infrastructure operations.

#5117. NEAR governance approves proposal to cut token issuance ceiling to 1.6%

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

NEAR's House of Stake passed HSP-029, which lowers the network's maximum annual issuance rate from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months.

NEAR Protocol's House of Stake governance body has approved a proposal to lower the network's maximum annual issuance rate from 2.5% to 1.6% over 24 months.

HSP-029, which passed the ballot on Monday with overwhelming support, retains the existing 90/10 split of newly issued tokens between staking participants and the protocol treasury, and changes only that single protocol parameter. This would not alter the emissions mechanism.

Once implemented, the issuance ceiling declines each epoch linearly at roughly 3.75 basis points per month, reaching the 1.6% target at the 24th month. This is essentially to slow dilution and reduce long-term sell pressure of the NEAR token NEAR Protocol (NEAR), as the network said its bootstrapping phase is largely over.

"This is how NEAR's economics evolve transparently and programmatically: token holders put a core parameter to a public vote, and the new rate rolls out epoch by epoch on a published schedule," NEAR said in an X post. "For NEAR's tokenomics, that means lower long-term issuance and a supply schedule anyone can verify."

The proposal estimates the ramp would avoid roughly 11.8 million NEAR of issuance over the first two years and 66.1 million over six years, leaving the supply about 4.36% smaller than under the existing 2.5% ceiling. Daily issuance would fall from approximately 89,500 NEAR to 57,300 NEAR at full implementation, while gross staking yield is projected to decline from 5.268% to 3.372% over the same period, assuming the staking participation rate measured in September remains unchanged.

For the proposal to go into full effect, the House of Stake Security Council will need to review the result before ratification. A 90-day grace period follows, after which validators must adopt a protocol upgrade for the change to become binding. NEAR previously cut the maximum issuance from 5% to 2.5% in October 2025.

#5116. The Women’s Sports Leagues to Watch This Fall and Winter, from NWSL to Unrivaled

Everyone say: “Thank you, women’s sports.”

Do you feel that chill in the air? Feels like time to stay home in a cozy sweatshirt and watch some women’s sports. Luckily, there are quite a few leagues to tune into right now, keeping us well fed through the winter.

Whether you’re a diehard fan of a team already or looking for something new to watch, these are the leagues we’re most excited about right now.

The National Women’s Soccer League (NWSL) season started back in March, but if you haven’t tuned in yet, now is the time. Playoffs start November 6, with New York/New Jersey Gotham FC, San Diego Wave, and Washington Spirit already having clinched a spot.

If you even slightly enjoyed watching the U.S. Men’s National Team in the World Cup this summer, you’ll love the NWSL. The teams are made up of the epic women who play on our national team, like Trinity Rodman, Rose Lavelle, and Mallory Swanson.

Even more women’s soccer: Ahead of the FIFA Women’s World Cup next summer, the U.S. Women’s National team is also playing a series of international games in the coming months, including a match against El Salvador on November 27.

How to watch: Friday matches air on Prime Video, Saturday matches air on ION, Paramount+, and ESPN, and Sunday matchups stream for free on the Victory+ platform. You can also watch all games on NWSL+.

It’s been 200 days since the UCLA Bruins beat the University of South Carolina Gamecocks in the national championship game last year, but who’s counting? College basketball finally returns on November 1 and will run through April, with teams like the University of South Carolina, University of Connecticut, and the University of Southern California starting the season in top-ranked positions.

Players to watch: It’s a little early to start talking national player of the year, but two women have already earned that spot in previous seasons (and are heavily favored to do so again). Sarah Strong of UConn won multiple awards to round out 2026, while JuJu Watkins of University of Southern California was national player of the year in 2025. Watkins is returning after missing an entire season due to a knee injury. Other early favorites include Hannah Hidalgo at Notre Dame and Mikayla Blakes at Vanderbilt.

How to watch: NCAA basketball games air across channels including ABC, Fox, CBS, ESPN, and you can watch either with cable or streaming apps like YouTube TV, Sling, Fubo, and Hulu + Live TV.

Speaking of basketball, Unrivaled is back—and bigger than ever—in January. The season starts on the 8th with a double-header game in Boston. Then, Unrivaled hits the road and will make stops across the country in New York City, Minneapolis, Los Angeles, Detroit, San Francisco, Houston, Philadelphia, and back to its home in Miami. As someone who had the pleasure of going to an Unrivaled game in person, let me tell you: If it’s coming to your city, go! (But, watching on TV is good too.)

How it’s different from the WNBA: Unrivaled has all the best WNBA players (like Napheesa Collier, Paige Bueckers, and Breanna Stewart), but the game setup is different. Games are 3-on-3 and faster-paced. Plus, the league is setting a new standard for women’s basketball: Players have equity in the league and don’t have to play abroad during their off seasons anymore.

How to watch: Unrivaled games air on HBO Max, Tru TV, and TNT.

League One Volleyball (LOVB, pronounced “love”) is about to begin its second season, increasing the number of teams from six to 10. The “First Serve” match is set to air on December 16, in San Antonio, Texas, when the reigning champions LOVB Austin will take on LOVB Nebraska.

Something cool about the league: LOVB invests in athletes of all ages, connecting youth and junior clubs all the way up to the pros and investing in their development.

How to watch: You can watch all games on YouTube, as well as stream them on demand on USA, ESPN+, and Victory+.

#5114. How T7X Is Building the Infrastructure for a New Era of Capital Markets

How T7X Is Building the Infrastructure for a New Era of Capital Markets

Most tokenized stocks aren't natively issued on a blockchain. They begin as conventional shares, sit with a broker or custodian, and are then represented by tokens issued separately. In effect, the market has largely replicated traditional securities structures on-chain instead of rethinking them from the ground up. The token "may track the value of the underlying shares," writes Ivan Kan, CMO of tokenization platform T7X, in an essay shared with Decrypt, "but the token holder is not necessarily the shareholder of record."

While the holder owns the price movement, whether they own the share is another matter altogether. The SEC set out three models in January: issuers tokenizing their own shares, third parties issuing tokens against shares held in custody, and firms issuing a separate security for synthetic exposure.

Writing for Forbes' Technology Council in August, Kan cited Pantera Capital research covering 593 tokenized assets across 11 asset classes, of which 77.6% were what Pantera called "digital wrappers around traditional infrastructure" rather than securities in their own right.

"What does the token legally represent, and who authorized it?" Kan asks, setting out an alternative model that T7X Equities is pursuing, which they dub "native issuance." A security exists because an issuer authorizes it, they argue, and "blockchain does not eliminate those corporate and securities-law requirements."

Under the native issuance model, the token is part of the authorized issuance from the start, running from issuer authorization to investor verification to transfer-agent validation, aiming at "one security, one authorized issuance, one ownership record, one digital representation."

Wyoming, Delaware, and Nevada corporate law recognizes a "certificate token," allowing shares to be represented electronically on a blockchain. T7X is incorporated in Wyoming and structures its issuances to operate within this framework. The blockchain then becomes "part of the infrastructure supporting the capitalization table" instead of being just another instrument layered on top.

The transfer agent keeps the official master securityholder file and the blockchain records transactions, while the legal record of ownership stays where regulators expect it.

In T7X's administrated offerings, that transfer agent is T7X Equities. Transfer agents register with the SEC and hold the authoritative record of who owns a security, processing transfers and maintaining the master file, which puts the company inside the model it is advocating as well as behind it. Its first example is Tranquil Healthcare Fund I, a Tier 2 Regulation A+ offering of up to $75 million that the SEC qualified on June 29. T7X Equities issues tokens representing its shares on Trusted Smart Chain and delivers them to investors' wallets, the company said.

"There's a registered, SEC-regulated gatekeeper that determines who owns which security at any point in time," T7X Chief Executive Pablo Penaloza said at Consensus in Miami earlier this year. "As long as you have a transfer agent involved, there is no way that securities can disappear without trace."

Kan calls the broader category "RWA+." The plus doesn't refer to any single rule, he argues, but the requirement that "compliance and oversight are real," and it rests on three elements. Native issuance creates the token as part of the security's authorized issuance. Integrated compliance writes investor verification and disclosure into the instrument from the start, not as an afterthought. Legally enforceable ownership puts the token holder on the transfer agent's record.

"A receipt is not an asset," Kan wrote in Forbes. "It is a claim wrapped around one, dependent on an intermediary to honor it." Synthetic "PreStocks" tied to SpaceX and Anthropic were never valid claims, he noted, because neither company authorized the transfers. Done properly, he argues, "ownership, compliance and settlement are the same fact."

Whether a token carries the rights of a share is becoming the dividing line elsewhere, too. The SEC's new innovation exemption covers tokenized stocks carrying dividends and voting rights while excluding price-tracking synthetics, and Nasdaq's arrangement with Kraken turns on tokens conveying the same voting rights as ordinary shares.

Penaloza pins the near-term opportunity on companies raising under $100 million, usually between $50 million and $75 million, through Regulation A offerings.

Regulation A+, Kan argues, suits this model because it permits public solicitation and non-accredited participation while requiring audited financials and continuing disclosure. Most tokenized assets rely instead on Regulation D placements, limited to accredited investors. Reg A also opens the door, Penaloza notes, to retirement accounts. Unregistered securities can legally sit in a self-directed IRA, but many brokers won't hold them and most investment advisers steer clients away. "The money is sitting in the older generations' retirement accounts," he said, and a registration attached to the investment is what makes an individual retiree comfortable holding it.

For investors already holding these securities, the benefit is an exit. Shareholders in Reg A and Reg D placements typically receive a paper certificate and then "have to go around and find somebody who will want to give them liquidity," Penaloza said. A native digital security can trade on a secondary market, he said, settling wallet to wallet without a custodian in between.

Giving Reg A and Reg D holdings somewhere to trade would, he argued, "incredibly speed up not only the rate of capital formation, but also the liquidity in the market." Within five years, he predicts, "the total tokenized portfolio will far surpass the existing market capitalization of what is currently trading on all the public markets."

To find out more about T7X’s real-world asset tokenization platform, head to t7x.com.

#5111. “My Runs Are Soft, Smooth, and, Once Again, Satisfying”

After three runs in the On Cloudsurfer 3, this writer finds her groove again.

I’ll let you in on a little secret: Until recently, I’d been dreading my runs. All the joy in them was gone. They felt more like a chore than an escape. They were just…hard. Every time out, my body felt heavy, my mind wandered, and staying in bed seemed so much more appealing. I needed to find my spark again: the glide, the levity, the freedom.

That’s why I decided to try On’s Cloudsurfer 3 for a few days. I was ready for a major change, something that would help me enjoy my runs again, and maybe this was it. After decades of logging miles, I wasn’t ready to accept that running wasn’t working for me anymore, mentally or physically. Luckily, after three of the softest running experiences I’ve ever had, I know I have still have many miles left in me.

Here’s my day-by-day account of testing the Cloudsurfer 3 across New York City parks, stairs, and hills:

I’ve been resting for a couple of days and my plan is to do a couple of easy miles. Unlike my other shoes, the Cloudsurfer 3 is cushioned—hyper-cushioned compared to anything that I have run in before. After lacing them up, I like how the upper mesh forms around my feet and makes them feel tucked in.

Then, I stand. Instantly I feel taller, which throws me off. I figure the extra height will translate into a soft ride, since that’s the whole point of the Surreal™ superfoam in the midsole and the geometry of the outsole. I know I’ll get used to it.

Once I’m outside, the moment of truth comes with my first propulsion. Immediately, everything seems lighter, smoother, softer. The extra height seems to be worth it. The levity and the spring from when I first started running are back; my gait is smoother than it’s been in a while, and I keep going. My plan to keep this run short gets smoked. The final tally is 5.25 practically effortless miles. When I return home, my legs don’t feel heavy and my post-workout yoga isn’t met with the normal “oohs” and “eeks.”

Last night, I dreamt about running. I can’t remember the last time that happened. My body feels good. No aches and pains like I usually have after my first run of the week. There might be something to a cushioned shoe after all. I decide to tackle some stairs and hills with a short out-and-back route to Central Park’s Bethesda Fountain stairs.

Running the steps, my toe strikes feel bouncy on the down phases and I’m able to get my knees up higher on the up. Both my heart rate and RPE rating—an 8 or 9 out of 10—tell me this is a tough workout. I climb the hill back home in a run-walk to recover, and this is when the rolling motion of the shoe feels more pronounced to me. The hill seems easier than in the past, and my heart rate recovers sooner. So I decide while exiting the park that I’m going to do streetlight intervals, alternating running and sprinting from one light to the next.

I dreamt about running again. Specifically, running Harlem Hill in the park. For those who aren’t familiar, this hill is a bear. Even though it’s only 0.3 miles, the incline can reach close to 8 percent at the top and averages around 4.5 percent. It’s brutal, and I haven’t run this hill since a half-marathon I did over a year ago, but I’m game to tackle it when I wake up. Yesterday’s runs piqued my curiosity about the Cloudsurfer 3’s performance on a more substantial incline.

Navigating the downhill switchbacks to get to the hill’s base is controlled and fast. I don’t feel like I’m pounding or lumbering down. Each step rolls from heel to toe, supported at every touchpoint. It’s a soft, smooth ride. But the real test of the day is still to come.

As I head up the hill, the roll continues to help propel me forward. The effort is greater than the day before (but so is the incline). There’s less pain than in previous passes of this run. When I get to the top I’m still winded, but my legs and glutes aren’t nearly as tired as I remember. I finish the second half of my run easily. When I get home, my overall pace says it all: 45 seconds faster per mile compared to day 1.

Thanks to Cloudsurfer 3, I found my running groove again.

Carey Rossi is the Senior News Editor for Prevention and Prevention.com. You can follow her @CareyRossi.

#5110. Liquid Network restores full bitcoin backing following $320 million exploit

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Samson Mow, a Liquid Federation board member, said Blockstream provided the missing bitcoin after the September exploit left nearly 600 BTC unreturned.

The Liquid Federation announced Tuesday that the sidechain's bitcoin reserves are back to full 1:1 backing for all LBTC, nearly two months after an exploit in which approximately $320 million in bitcoin was withdrawn from the network.

Users have been able to swap Bitcoin (BTC) and LBTC again for several weeks through participating providers. Direct Federation peg-outs were previously restricted pending additional security checks, although some semi-automated peg-outs have since resumed. Tuesday's announcement did not say whether the remaining restrictions had been lifted. Reserve balances are published at liquid.network, where Liquid node operators can also verify the backing themselves.

"We extend our sincere appreciation to the Bitcoin community, Liquid users, and ecosystem partners for their patience, understanding, and continued engagement throughout the recovery process," the Liquid Federation said. "Your support and involvement has been instrumental as we worked to restore the network's reserves and strengthen its underlying infrastructure."

About 4,000 BTC was taken from Liquid's Federation wallet in the Sept. 6 exploit. The attacker sent back roughly 3,400 BTC the next day after Blockstream, the developer of Liquid Network's underlying technology, patched the affected bridge nodes, but withheld about 600 BTC. Adam Back-led Blockstream later rejected the attacker's demand for a 10% bug bounty in exchange for returning the remaining coins.

"We will not be a party to the precedent that open-source software developed for the good of the Bitcoin community should subject its developers to paying a ransom that far exceeds their economic participation," Blockstream said at the time.

Liquid Federation board member and former Blockstream executive Samson Mow reported a 50 BTC deposit into the Federation wallet on Oct. 28, leaving 550 BTC outstanding. Another 300 BTC arrived on Monday, followed by a further 250 BTC later that day, bringing the reserves back to full backing. Mow identified Blockstream as the source of the funds. "As stated many times, Blockstream," he wrote on X when asked where the bitcoin was coming from.

Liquid's September incident report traced the exploit to a bug involving cached range proof verifications in Elements, the underlying software used to run the Liquid Network. The attacker was able to create approximately 4,000 unbacked LBTC, which were then used to withdraw bitcoin through Liquid Network wallet and trading platform SideSwap, a Federation member with a peg-out authorization key. Liquid said its network operators were not hacked and no keys were compromised.

Elements v23.3.4 was released on Sept. 9 to address the vulnerability. Liquid resumed producing blocks later that day, with user transactions returning on Sept. 10, although Federation peg-outs remained disabled at that stage.

In its latest update, the Federation said it was preparing changes to governance and oversight, as well as additional technical and operational safeguards. It expects to share further details in the coming weeks.

#5107. Bitmine Buys More Ethereum as Tom Lee Calls the Binance Hack Bullish

Bitmine Buys More Ethereum as Tom Lee Calls the Binance Hack Bullish

Bitmine Immersion Technologies kept its weekly Ethereum buying streak intact, adding 8,573 ETH worth roughly $19 million, while chairman Tom Lee used the record Binance hack to argue that crypto has grown more resilient, not more fragile.

The NYSE-listed company said Monday its holdings now total 6,060,506 ETH, worth roughly $13.7 billion at a reference price of $2,261 per coin.

That represents 4.9% of Ethereum’s 122.2 million token supply, leaving Bitmine, by its own count, 99% of the way to its goal of owning 5% of the network, a target it brands the “Alchemy of 5%,” reached in just 15 months. The firm has bought Ethereum every week since launching its treasury strategy on June 30, 2025.

Most of Lee’s commentary focused on last week’s roughly $6.4 billion theft from Binance, which the FBI has attributed to North Korea. Lee called it “a terrible development” but argued the market’s “relatively muted” reaction was “very constructive.” (Disclosure: Tom Lee is one of several investors in Dastan, Decrypt’s parent company.)

Lee noted that when North Korea’s Lazarus-linked hackers drained $1.5 billion from Bybit in February 2025, Ethereum fell 8% that day and 20% within a week, whereas ETH slid just 4% after the Binance breach, despite the theft being roughly four times larger.

Lee laid out six reasons for the muted response: Binance’s ability to cover all customer losses, the absence of a cascade of DeFi failures like the one that followed Bybit, swift industry action that cut the potential loss in half, the FBI naming North Korea within two days rather than the months such attributions have taken historically, the fact that the exploit was a “corporate infiltration” of Binance’s infrastructure rather than a blockchain flaw, and the limited price downside itself, which he read as a sign a “crypto bull market is underway.”

Taken together, he said, the episode shows the industry is “increasingly resilient and more capable of absorbing these nation-state assaults.”

Counting cash, other tokens and what it calls “moonshot” investments, Bitmine’s total holdings stood at $14.5 billion as of Nov. 1, including 216 Bitcoin, $498 million in cash and marketable securities, a $180 million stake in Beast Industries and an $82 million position in Eightco Holdings, which it bills as a rare public proxy for indirect OpenAI exposure.

Bitmine remains the world’s largest Ethereum treasury and the second-largest crypto treasury overall, trailing only Michael Saylor’s Strategy, which the release said holds about 848,517 BTC.

Strategy recently resumed its own Bitcoin buying with a $66 million purchase after a brief pause.

Bitmine has 5,227,037 ETH, about 86% of its holdings, staked through its MAVAN platform, with projected annualized staking revenue of roughly $358 million.

#5104. Ethereum Layer 2 Aztec gets Tierra V6 upgrade to support faster private apps

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Tierra V6 fixes a critical V5 security issue and speeds up private transaction proofs, with new apps launching or preparing to launch on Aztec.

The upgrade resolves vulnerabilities found in earlier versions, including a critical issue in V5, the foundation said. It also adds a new AI-based system for security testing.

When asked why the critical issue wasn't fixed sooner, an Aztec spokesperson said the fix required the whole network to move to a new version.

"Because Aztec is a decentralized network, if a critical vulnerability is discovered, the entire network must upgrade to the new version to implement the fix," the spokesperson told The Block.

Aztec has no central operator that can make the change on its own, the spokesperson said, adding that there are plans to make future upgrades faster if vulnerabilities are discovered.

Aztec said its network has more than 3,000 validators, no centralized sequencer, and no privileged viewing keys.

Besides the security fixes, Tierra makes private transaction proofs twice as fast, taking one second to generate on a native device, according to the foundation.

Messages from Ethereum can now reach Aztec in as little as 12 seconds, compared with an average of two minutes before, the foundation said, noting that the change speeds up deposits and other transactions between the networks.

Developers on Aztec can choose which functions of their apps are private and which are public, the foundation said, adding that private functions run and generate proofs on the user's device, while public functions run on the network.

The upgrade comes shortly after Aztec Labs relaunched zk.money, a self-custodial wallet for private crypto payments, three years after discontinuing the original product.

"With Aztec Tierra and zk.money, users can deposit directly from any Ethereum wallet and make lightning-fast private transactions without complicated bridging workflows," Joe Andrews, co-founder and CEO of Aztec Labs, said in a statement.

Other apps launching or preparing to launch include Galactica for private AI inference, Nemi for private perpetuals trading, and the private browser wallet Nulo, according to the announcement.

Xochi supports private intents and trading, while Nyx lets users make private payments to any Ethereum wallet. Olla offers liquid staking for the Aztec token.

Rolling Chat, a private message board based on Ethereum co-founder Vitalik Buterin's Aztec experiments, is also on the list.

Aztec also introduced a new brand and website alongside Tierra, describing its aim as becoming the platform for "unstoppable private apps."

#5100. Binance says hackers used fake job interview, internal AI tool in $6.3 billion breach

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyber threats in 2026, including similar attempts involving fraudulent interviews and malicious software.

Binance said hackers used a fraudulent job interview to compromise an employee’s computer, then used an internal AI tool to access its wallet infrastructure and steal more than $6.3 billion in digital assets.

In an initial incident report published Tuesday, the world's largest crypto exchange detailed how attackers used malware and stolen credentials to access its wallet-signing systems. Binance said it has frozen or intercepted about $2.85 billion in assets and replenished 98% of impacted assets.

"This wasn't one mistake or one breach — it was a months-long campaign. We're publishing the full report because the lessons go far beyond Binance," co-CEO Richard Teng said on X. "The speed of coordination could not be matched in traditional finance."

Binance said the FBI had linked the attack to North Korea, while its own investigation found that hackers used social engineering, malware, and compromised internal AI tooling to gain access to its wallet-signing infrastructure and authorize unauthorized transactions.

According to the report, the attackers first targeted a Wallet DevOps engineer in June through a tampered Homebrew installation downloaded while he was trying to install an AI coding tool. Binance said its security team detected the anomaly, replaced the device, and rotated the affected credentials, but the incident exposed information about the engineer's role and colleagues. In September, attackers then targeted another employee through a fraudulent recruitment process, posing as company executives before tricking him into downloading a malicious coding assignment that infected his work computer and gave hackers unauthorized access.

On Oct. 20, the attackers used the compromised employee's computer and an internal AI tool to gain control of two other engineers' devices, discovering a container deployment permission on one of them. Nine days later, they used the stolen credentials to deploy a container and run a malicious script that launched the wallet attack.

Binance said it detected and prevented 57 suspected North Korea-linked cyberattacks throughout 2026, many involving similar tactics such as fraudulent interviews, malicious developer tools, and social engineering. On Monday, North Korean leader Kim Jong-un's sister called FBI claims linking North Korea to the hack a "grave political provocation."

Binance said it has since closed the entry points used in the attack, rebuilt or replaced affected systems, strengthened device controls, and expanded AI-based behavioral anomaly detection.

Separately, the exchange continued restoring normal operations, reopening withdrawals in phases for dozens of assets across BSC, Ethereum, Solana, XRP, Sui, Arbitrum, Optimism, TRON and Base networks starting at 4 a.m. UTC on Nov. 3. Binance said it was prioritizing tokens based on user demand over the past week. The exchange later reported that hourly deposits exceeded withdrawals, describing the shift as a sign that flows were stabilizing and user activity was improving.

#5097. CFTC approves individual stock perpetual futures trading in US for the first time

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

Perpetual futures contracts surged in popularity over the past year, particularly after disruptions in global supply chains in the Middle East earlier this year at the onset of the war.

The Commodity Futures Trading Commission approved the ability for exchanges in the United States to offer perpetual futures contracts tied to individual stocks — just months after those assets were put into the spotlight by President Donald Trump.

On Tuesday, CFTC Chair Michael Selig greenlit the ability to trade those perpetual futures contracts for the first time in the U.S. for platforms.

"This expansion marks a significant step towards allowing U.S. market participants, consistent with applicable law, to avail themselves of the advantages of perpetual futures that have led to their emergence as widely sought tools for risk-management and price-discovery," Selig said.

Perpetuals, or perps, are a type of futures contract that don't have an expiration date and allow people to bet on the price movement of assets without owning them directly.

Perpetual futures contracts surged in popularity over the past year, particularly after disruptions in global supply chains in the Middle East earlier this year at the onset of the war, after the U.S. and Israel conducted missile strikes on Iran, which triggered supply shocks. That happened over the weekend, so traditional oil futures markets were closed, but platforms like Hyperliquid operate 24/7, so much of the trading happened onchain.

Over the past year, the CFTC has approved a few perpetual futures contracts. The agency first opened the door for bitcoin perpetual futures contracts in the U.S. when its staff greenlit KalshiEX and Coinbase to list the products. Then in June, the CFTC released a request for comment on crude oil perpetual contracts and 24/7 trading.

Perpetuals futures contracts have also caught Trump's attention. In August, he said he wanted the CFTC to work to bring Hyperliquid into the U.S.

Both the SEC and CFTC would oversee the contracts, Selig said on Tuesday.

"This framework recognized the complementary roles of the CFTC and SEC in overseeing American financial markets by providing a pathway for innovation at the intersection of the U.S.," Selig said.

#5094. Brazil's Largest Exchange Mercado Bitcoin Halts Withdrawals After $40 Million Hack

Brazil's Largest Exchange Mercado Bitcoin Halts Withdrawals After $40 Million Hack

Brazil's largest crypto exchange, Mercado Bitcoin, suspended deposits and withdrawals on Monday after identifying a security incident in one of its systems in the early hours of the morning. The exchange said no customer lost money and no data leaked.

"We identified and quickly contained a security incident in one of our systems early this morning," Mercado Bitcoin said in a Portuguese-language statement on X. "As a preventive protection measure, deposits and withdrawals are temporarily suspended."

Blockchain security firm Hypernative flagged more than $40 million in suspicious transfers that may be linked to the incident, according to a preliminary analysis that spans at least 10 blockchains, including Bitcoin, Ethereum, Solana, and Tron. Mercado Bitcoin has not confirmed that figure and has not disclosed how the attackers got in.

In a follow-up post, the exchange published the destination addresses it identified on five networks: EVM chains (blockchains built to run the same code as Ethereum), Bitcoin, Solana, Stellar, and Tron. It said the addresses were released only so others can identify and track the funds on-chain.

Reforçamos que:• Não houve nenhum prejuízo aos clientes;• Não houve vazamento de dados.

Como medida preventiva de proteção, depósitos e saques…

— Mercado Bitcoin (@MercadoBitcoin) November 2, 2026

Tether CEO Paolo Ardoino said in a post on X that the stablecoin issuer has frozen more than 4.5 million USDT connected to the incident.

USDT is a dollar-pegged stablecoin, and Tether can blacklist the addresses holding it so the tokens cannot be moved, though a freeze does not by itself return the funds to their owner.

The incident comes days after hackers drained more than $6 billion from Binance on October 29, a theft the FBI attributed to North Korea's TraderTraitor group. Binance has since reopened withdrawals and said it replenished about 95% of the affected assets. Mercado Bitcoin has not linked the two incidents and has not said who is behind its own.

Deposits and withdrawals at Mercado Bitcoin remain suspended, and the exchange has not given a date for resuming them. It said it will post regular updates on X.

There are warmups before practice, conditioning sessions, gym workouts, recovery afterwards, and, of course, the inevitable post-game team hangouts while you’re still wearing half your soccer gear. The right off-field pieces can make all of that a little more comfortable.

Brooks has plenty of options that can slot into those in-between moments—from cushioned running shoes for off-field training to lightweight layers for chilly practices to comfortable sweats for the ride home.

After spending 90 minutes running around in soccer socks, changing into a fresh pair can feel not only like a luxury, but a necessity. #5090. Justin Sun seeks sanctions against World Liberty, alleging Trump-linked firm hid UAE ties

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

The dispute over World Liberty's citizenship emerged after the company amended an initial court disclosure that had listed only one entity in its ownership chain and Florida as its sole citizenship.

Tron founder Justin Sun said Tuesday he has filed a motion seeking sanctions against World Liberty Financial, alleging the DeFi protocol failed to disclose its United Arab Emirates citizenship until months into their legal dispute.

Sun, who says he invested $45 million in WLFI tokens, sued the Trump-linked crypto venture in April, accusing it of fraud and unlawfully freezing his tokens.

The dispute over World Liberty's citizenship emerged after the company amended an initial court disclosure that had listed only one entity in its ownership chain and Florida as its sole citizenship. In a subsequent filing, World Liberty identified 22 members in its ownership chain and claimed citizenship in Florida, Delaware, Puerto Rico, and the UAE, according to Sun.

Sun alleges the late disclosure was an attempt to challenge the federal court's jurisdiction and push the case out of open court. World Liberty was "proudly" American when selling tokens, he said, but discovered its UAE roots "only when it needed a way out of an American federal courtroom."

Under federal diversity jurisdiction rules, Sun argued, his case could proceed in federal court only if he and World Liberty were citizens of different jurisdictions. Because a company takes on the citizenship of its owners for these purposes, World Liberty's UAE ties could undermine the court's jurisdiction.

Sun said he plans to refile the lawsuit in California state court after the sanctions motion is resolved.

In May, World Liberty Financial countersued Sun, accusing him of running a "meritless PR stunt" against the Trump-family-backed crypto project after his tokens were frozen.

World Liberty debuted in October 2024 amid significant scrutiny because of its association with President Donald Trump and his family. The WLFI token reached an all-time high of around $0.33 and currently trades near $0.051, giving it a fully diluted valuation of approximately $5.1 billion.

#5088. Build Strong Bones in Just Four Weeks With This Plan: Download the PDF Today

Bulletproof your bones with this easy-to-follow workout plan.

With aging comes a number of changes—from shifts in muscle mass and hormones to changes in sleep and bone health. Bone density naturally begins to decline around age 35, but there’s plenty you can do to support strong, healthy bones at every stage of life.

Introducing the WH+ 4 Weeks to Stronger Bones workout plan. This comprehensive program gives you the tools to build strength, support bone density, and keep your body resilient at any age.

Each week includes three strength workouts, two bone-health-focused cardio circuits (one optional), one active recovery day, and one rest day. The typical schedule will look something like this:

Saturday: Bone-Strength Circuit 2 (optional)

Each workout is designed to challenge your major muscle groups and boost your bone health in the process. All workouts are outlined in the PDF, including:

Download the PDF below, and save it to your computer or phone for easy access. Then, use it to follow your workouts, log your numbers, and watch your strength build over time.

The only way to truly build bone strength is to load your bones consistently. Repetition is what helps them become stronger over time, so it’s important for you to find a workout schedule that you know you’ll stick with. Although each workout is assigned to a specific day, feel free to adjust the schedule to fit your life. What matters most is choosing workout days that are realistic for you and following through.

Your body—and bones!—need the right fuel to thrive. Bones are made up of a mix of calcium and other nutrients (in fact, 99 percent of your body’s calcium is stored in your bones!). That makes a nutrient-rich diet a vital part of supporting bone health. Use the nutrition cheat sheet included in this program to help you choose calcium-rich foods and build bone-friendly meals.

Fill in your workout charts as you complete each session. As the weeks go by, pay attention to how the exercises feel, and choose more challenging weights and exercises when you’re ready—and watch your strength, balance, and power all improve. Each gain is a sign your hard work is paying off, and that you’re making meaningful steps toward stronger bones.

#5087. Prediction markets bet on Democratic sweep of House, Senate in midterm elections

Get The Block’s latest crypto news and price alerts for the coins you follow, right in Chrome. Add our free extension here.

On Tuesday, Polymarket users gave Democrats a 76% chance of securing the Senate, and the House a 94% chance.

Ahead of polls closing in the U.S. midterm elections, bettors on prediction market platforms are increasingly pricing in Democrats taking both the House and Senate.

As of early Tuesday afternoon, Polymarket users gave Democrats a 76% chance of securing the Senate, and the House a 94% chance. Similarly, on Kalshi, bettors said Democrats had a 94% chance of controlling the House and a 75% chance for the Senate. All 435 House seats are up for election, as well as 35 of the 100 Senate seats.

Prediction markets surged in popularity during the 2024 presidential election, emerging as an alternative barometer of public opinion and drawing comparisons to national polling. Since then, platforms like Polymarket and Kalshi have generated significant revenue and reached multibillion-dollar valuations.

But interest in election-related trading has cooled considerably since 2024, according to a Bloomberg report published Tuesday. Politics accounted for just 1% of trading volume on both Kalshi and Polymarket in October, down from more than 60% during the 2024 election, Bloomberg reported.

A shift in parties is pivotal since Democrats are likely to then lead key committees, driving the agenda. For crypto, that could mean that Democrats such as Elizabeth Warren and Sherrod Brown could lead panels overseeing the digital asset industry.

Experts who spoke with The Block last month said that would mean more oversight over key issues, including President Donald Trump's crypto wealth through his family's World Liberty Financial, and less of an emphasis on crypto legislation.

As for key crypto races, such as Ohio, where incumbent Jon Husted will face off against crypto critic and former Sen. Brown, bettors on Kalshi say Brown has a 71% chance of beating Husted. For other races, including lawmakers focused on crypto legislation, Sen. Cory Booker, D.-N.J. has a 98% chance on Kalshi.

#5086. Elon Musk Mocks Sam Altman Over Claim Home PCs Are Wasting RAM

Elon Musk Mocks Sam Altman Over Claim Home PCs Are Wasting RAM

Elon Musk has mocked OpenAI CEO Sam Altman for arguing that the world's limited supply of computer memory would be better used in AI data centers than in people's home computers.

Altman made the comments in an interview with the YouTube channel Cheeky Pint, while discussing the shortages of memory and other components caused by the AI boom.

"A lot of people want local inference, and somehow they assume that the problem is solved now because we're going to have open-source models," Altman said, referring to running AI models on a user's own hardware. "But from like a global allocation perspective, actually, it's quite terrible to have everybody have a bunch of the world's limited supply of DRAM sitting in their own computer and being used 5% of the time."

"You want maximum utilization in a cloud if you're trying to deal with this very supply-constrained environment," he added. "And somehow, many people's intuition is the opposite of that."

Musk responded on X on Sunday with a meme showing Hans Landa, the Nazi officer played by Christoph Waltz in the film Inglourious Basterds, captioned: "You are withholding DRAM from the cloud, are you not?" "The people deserve to have their RAM too!" Musk wrote alongside the image.

The people deserve to have their RAM too! pic.twitter.com/q6dwauALw4

In the interview, Altman blamed the shortage partly on memory makers' caution. For years, a DRAM producer "would invest $20 billion of CapEx to make $19 billion," he said, and companies that nearly went bankrupt building too many factories in past cycles have been slow to expand, even as they now stand to generate huge profits.

Altman said he would like to see prices for energy, chips and the rest of the supply chain fall. "If it doesn't go down, I'll be very sad," he said, adding that for prices to fall, supply needs to grow.

The jab is the latest in a long-running feud between the two men, who co-founded OpenAI with others in 2015 before Musk left in 2018.

Musk sued OpenAI and Altman in 2024, accusing them of abandoning the organization's founding nonprofit mission. In May, a federal jury in California rejected the $150 billion lawsuit, finding OpenAI, Altman and president Greg Brockman not liable on all claims. OpenAI's lawyers had argued that the suit was motivated in part by competition, after Musk launched rival AI company xAI in 2023.

#5081. Nvidia and Nike Lead Biggest Wave Yet of Tokenized Stock Objections

Nvidia and Nike Lead Biggest Wave Yet of Tokenized Stock Objections

Even Nvidia doesn’t want its shares on OKX and ICE’s planned tokenized stock venue.

The chipmaker is among 12 new companies listed as objecting issuers in an updated notice from OKXICE TSV dated Friday. The others are Costco, Nike, Micron, CrowdStrike, Palo Alto Networks, Reddit, Hewlett Packard Enterprise, Corning, Bloom Energy, IREN, and USA Rare Earth. The additions bring the total to 50 companies.

It’s the largest single batch yet. The list stood at 38 in the venue’s Oct. 28 notice, when Walmart and Eli Lilly joined, and at nine when the first objectors appeared on Oct. 15. It has more than quintupled since.

The newcomers span chips, cybersecurity, retail, and energy. Nvidia, Micron, and Corning join hardware peers such as AMD, Intel, and Broadcom, while CrowdStrike and Palo Alto Networks bring cybersecurity heavyweights to the list. IREN, a Bitcoin miner that has expanded into AI cloud computing, adds a name from the crypto industry itself.

Under the SEC’s innovation exemption, which the agency unveiled in September after the Clarity Act stalled in the Senate, a venue must notify a company before offering a version of its shares tokenized by an unaffiliated third party. If the company objects in writing within 30 days, the venue can’t make that stock available for trading, according to the SEC’s order. The objection applies only to that venue.

OKXICE TSV, a 50-50 joint venture between NYSE owner Intercontinental Exchange and OKX’s U.S. holding company, plans to let approved users trade tokenized U.S. stocks around the clock. Trades would run through permissioned Uniswap v4 pools on OKX’s XLayer blockchain, paired with stablecoins such as USDC.

None of the objecting companies have explained their decisions, but Nasdaq offered one possible rationale when it opted its own shares out of the exemption.

A spokesperson told The Wall Street Journal that Nasdaq doesn’t want its stock trading on a venue that doesn’t show the best prices in real time or report trades to the consolidated tape.

Further, Crypto in America reported that Nasdaq may be playing a role behind the scenes in encouraging companies to opt out of the OKX-ICE venture. “Our Nasdaq rep had reached out to us regarding OKX listing, suggesting we should object and that they would hold a call to discuss why,” an executive at a Nasdaq-listed company told the publication. “Nasdaq is definitely partly behind this,” the exec reportedly said.

OKXICE’s notice acknowledges it isn’t subject to Regulation NMS, the rules designed to ensure investors get the best displayed prices. It also warns that broker-dealers face unsettled questions about trade reporting and best execution on the venue.

Some of the market’s biggest names, including Apple, Tesla and Amazon, still haven’t appeared on the list.

#5079. FATF targets crypto hubs in review of money laundering controls

The watchdog has not named the jurisdictions under review. Initial findings on compliance with its crypto standards are due in June 2027.

Update (Nov. 3, 2026, 7:35 am UTC): This article has been updated to include comments from an FATF spokesperson and additional details from its July 2026 report.

The Financial Action Task Force (FATF) is launching a targeted review of jurisdictions with significant crypto activity to address gaps in how governments apply global anti-money laundering standards.

On Friday, the FATF, an intergovernmental body that sets global standards to combat money laundering, announced that the review would focus primarily on jurisdictions with “materially important” virtual asset service provider (VASP) activity, taking their existing assessment status into account.

A  FATF spokesperson pointed to its July 2026 report, which lists FATF members and other jurisdictions with significant VASP activity. Jurisdictions in the table, including the US, UK, Singapore and the United Arab Emirates account for about 97% of the global virtual asset market.

The spokesperson did not confirm which jurisdictions from the existing list would be selected. Initial findings will be published in June 2027, with further results in October 2027.

For non-member jurisdictions, inclusion in the table requires a more than 0.25% of global crypto trading volume or a top-30 ranking for crypto ownership and adoption.

Inclusion in the table does not indicate a jurisdiction’s illicit finance risk or compliance with FATF standards.

The July report found that 83% of surveyed jurisdictions had adopted crypto Travel Rule legislation, while significant enforcement gaps persisted.

The Travel Rule requires crypto service providers to share information about the sender and recipient of transfers.

The report found that 55 of the 91 jurisdictions with Travel Rule laws had yet to take supervisory or enforcement action over compliance.

Authorities also struggled to identify unlicensed crypto businesses and oversee providers serving local customers from overseas.

#5074. Binance taps half its SAFU Bitcoin holdings, releases reserve proof

Binance’s SAFU Bitcoin wallet now holds about 7,500 BTC after a 7,499 BTC transfer linked to the hack, with no timeline for restoring the $1 billion fund.

Binance’s publicly disclosed emergency Bitcoin wallet held roughly half its previous balance on Monday after the exchange tapped its user protection fund to cover losses from Thursday’s $6.3 billion hack.

The exchange confirmed a transfer of 7,499 Bitcoin (BTC) on Saturday, linking it to the partial deployment of its Secure Asset Fund for Users (SAFU).

As of Monday, the wallet held about 7,500 BTC, worth $583 million, down from about 15,000 BTC before the transfer.

The theft, the largest in cryptocurrency history, forced Binance to suspend withdrawals while it worked to replace the stolen assets. The FBI attributed the attack to North Korea on Saturday, identifying the hackers as TraderTraitor.

Binance began reopening withdrawals in phases on Sunday and said it had replenished approximately 95% of the affected assets, up from 90% a day earlier. It continued buying the remaining, less-liquid tokens through market and over-the-counter transactions.

The exchange is using its corporate treasury, SAFU and part of its futures insurance fund to cover the losses.

Binance’s SAFU policy outlines regular monitoring and rebalancing of the fund’s market value.

“If the fund’s market value falls below US $800 million due to BTC price fluctuations, Binance will rebalance the fund to restore its value to $1 billion,” it states.

Related: Binance hackers begin swapping stolen Solana tokens for Bitcoin: Chainalysis

The policy does not specify a timeline for replenishing funds withdrawn following a security incident.

Binance did not immediately respond to Cointelegraph’s questions about SAFU’s remaining reserves, further deployments and plans to replenish the fund.

Binance said Monday that users could now verify their balances against its Saturday proof-of-reserves snapshot after it completed the accompanying cryptographic proof.

The exchange said the snapshot showed customer holdings in 10 cryptocurrencies, including Bitcoin, Ether and USDT, were backed at least 1:1.

The exchange plans to take another snapshot covering more tokens on Wednesday, with the accompanying proof expected to take another 40 to 50 hours to generate.

#5073. Proton Users Revolt After CEO Calls Security Caution 'Loser Thinking' Amid AI Push

Proton Users Revolt After CEO Calls Security Caution 'Loser Thinking' Amid AI Push

Proton users are reporting subscription cancellations after revelations that CEO Andy Yen pressured engineers to embrace AI and dismissed a request for more security testing as “loser thinking.”

The backlash cuts into Proton’s main selling point—trusting it with information people want kept private. Its encrypted email service scrambles messages to protect their contents, and customers questioning its development practices are asking whether faster releases could come at their expense.

A Proton employee asked the company in August for a few days to pentest new code, meaning specialists would try to break into it before release, per 404 Media. Yen replied: “That’s bullshit and loser thinking. Of course it’s possible to be fast and secure.”

He also urged staff to stop writing code by hand and use more AI, and mentioned that he’d be reinterviewing its staff to see if and how they are using AI to do their job, the outlet reported. Its investigation drew on nine current and former employees, internal messages, and meeting recordings.

This didn’t land well among users, and customers have responded with cancellation notices and demands for new leadership on social media such as Reddit. A proposal on Proton’s feedback forum calling for Yen to step down had 2,020 votes when reviewed, alongside comments from users saying they had canceled subscriptions or would not renew.

Besides the blind use of AI, some former and current Proton employees on Reddit and social media have documented a toxic work culture, especially from the CEO.

Those posts document anger, rather than a verified count of departing customers. Canceling a paid plan also does not necessarily mean deleting an account.

Proton’s AI ambitions predate this dispute. Its Lumo chatbot comes with protections against using conversations to train AI, but internal use of AI was not among Proton’s selling points, even in the peak of the chatbot hype.

To address concerns over irresponsible use of AI, on November 1, technology chief Bart Butler published an engineering manifesto arguing that developers should direct large language models, AI systems that generate text and code, rather than treat them as occasional assistants.

Butler compared manual coding to memorizing phone numbers, and envisioned eventually ending most human code review. He also insisted that expertise and testing remain essential, with engineers accountable for work that models cannot reliably handle.

“Your job has fundamentally changed. It is no longer to write code, and LLMs are not here to help you write better code faster,” the manifesto reads. “Your job is to help the LLM write better code faster.” Doubling on its stance, Proton sees avoiding AI as an “existential threat” to the success of its very mission.

“‘Programming’ is dead. Engineering is not,” Buttler told his own team. “Your old job is gone”.

The document also calls for company-wide investment in automation, arguing that generating code faster achieves little if approvals and other development steps remain slow. Butler described that overhaul as the next phase of adoption.

AI tools can introduce security problems of their own. For example, Microsoft researchers said a now patched Claude flaw could expose secret access credentials through malicious instructions hidden in material the coding assistant reviewed.

They can also help find weaknesses: Google’s PageBreak system searches its applications for exploitable bugs and checks whether attacks actually work.

Proton acknowledged to 404 Media that management had sometimes communicated too bluntly and that employees were anxious. Its public manifesto nevertheless argues that failing to adopt AI quickly threatens the company’s ability to compete.

Yen canceled the re-interviews on October 30. The assessments would have required staff to explain their AI use, with employees fearing their answers could determine whether they kept their jobs.

“Generally, bone density loss starts extremely slowly and then accelerates around the time of menopause,” says Carl Herndon, MD, an orthopedic surgeon at NewYork-Presbyterian/Columbia University Irving Medical Center. “Then, in the early post-menopause phase of life, it continues to decrease but at a slower rate.”

With the right tactics, you can help preserve—and even improve—bone density while building the strength, balance, and power that supports healthy aging.
